AI Engineering Roadmap

Build, evaluate, and operate grounded AI applications — 6 tiers, 19 prerequisite-based Levels.

Designed for Abdelrahman’s Node.js/Express backend experience, Python fundamentals, and SQL background. Based on the official ITI Native AI Developer syllabus, three selected AI Engineering books, and Anthropic’s Context Engineering article. Independent study guide; not affiliated with or endorsed by ITI.

Start here · Coverage matrix · Book reading order · Chapter lookup · Capstone · Progress · Sources

How the repository works

File Purpose
Each numbered Level/README.md The ordered learning journey: one primary resource, exact reading, task and completion check per step.
Each numbered Level/resources.md The full retained Arabic/English library, with formats, roles, selected portions and scope limits.
Each numbered Level/details.md Diagnostics, complete technical scope, debugging guidance, self-check answers and ITI provenance.
curriculum.json Machine-readable Levels, dependencies, hours, ITI mapping, and exercises.
progress/tracker.csv Your status, evidence, and diagnostic waivers; generated dashboard in PROGRESS.md.
project/milestones Eight incremental acceptance contracts for one capstone.

Reading the roadmap

Label Meaning
ITI N01–N32 Local identifiers for exact titles from the published Native AI Developer brochure.
Industry extension Specific tools and operational depth added by this roadmap; not an official ITI requirement.
Difficulty 1–5 Relative learning complexity: introductory → advanced applied work; not seniority.
Personal hours Focused learning plus assigned implementation; not ITI classroom hours and not a guarantee.
P0 / P1 / P2 / P3 Essential now / first complete project / production quality / advanced or optional.

Topic-level learning resources

Open a Level and follow Start Here / Recommended Learning Path. Every numbered step connects the topic → assigned book slice → one Primary resource → practical task → completion check. Consult details.md for diagnostics and technical reference; open resources.md only when you need an alternative or implementation reference.

The complete Arabic/Egyptian and English libraries are preserved. Resource Type distinguishes a single video, playlist, full/short course, documentation, book section and lab; Role tells you whether to follow it or consult it. You do not need to complete every listed resource.

Learning-experience audit · Resource policy · All-Level coverage audit · Reference comparison

Choose one explanation per gap and build. The expanded library contains alternatives; it does not increase the required path to every listed course.

Your starting advantage

Already practiced Review quickly Learn deliberately
Express REST, middleware, validation, JWT/RBAC, Jest/Supertest Pass L00 diagnostic and map concepts to Python Typing versus runtime validation; Pydantic, dependency lifecycle, pytest fixtures.
Python syntax/OOP and SQL concepts Skip a full beginner course after diagnostic evidence Async I/O, SQLAlchemy sessions, migrations, cancellation, ownership-aware retrieval.
Git and Docker basics Demonstrate a clean setup and container build Evaluation gates, prompt/data versions, traces, secrets, rollback.

The complete roadmap

Follow prerequisites, not a weekly calendar. Build from L01; do not wait to finish all reading. Foundations L02/L03 are bounded labs, not a Data Science specialization.

Tier 1 · Engineering and Model Foundations

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L00 · Baseline Assessment and Product Scope N01, N03, N09, N30, N31 Identify transferable backend skills and specific gaps. Define users, requirements, failure cases, and acceptance evidence. 1/5 Diagnostic 3–5 h
L01 · Python, FastAPI, and Service Testing N02, N05, N07 Use type hints, Pydantic, dependency injection, and lifespan management. Separate HTTP handlers from application logic and test the service. 2/5 L00 14–20 h
L02 · Practical Mathematics, ML, and Data Preparation N11, N12, N13 Understand vectors, matrices, similarity, probability, loss, and gradients through small examples. Run an ML baseline with correct splits, preprocessing, and evaluation. Recognize leakage and distinguish a similarity score from a calibrated probability. 2/5 L00 16–24 h
L03 · Deep Learning, Foundation Models, and Serving N14, N15, N16 Distinguish pretraining, supervised fine-tuning, preference tuning, and inference. Understand layers, autograd, attention, tokenization, sampling, and resource constraints. Choose an external provider or local inference endpoint with a reasoned boundary. 3/5 L00 12–18 h

Tier 2 · Reliable LLM Application Basics

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L04 · Prompt Engineering, Structured Outputs, and LLM Integration N04, N16 Design instructions, examples, schemas, and untrusted-input boundaries. Integrate one model behind an adapter with controlled error handling. 2/5 L01, L03 10–16 h
L05 · Evaluation Engineering N05, N20 Define datasets and rubrics before expanding the application. Distinguish deterministic tests, component evaluation, and end-to-end quality. 3/5 L04 14–22 h
L06 · PostgreSQL, ORM, and AI Application State N06, N07, N09 Move from Mongoose-style ODM experience to SQLAlchemy relations, sessions, transactions, and Alembic. Design document, conversation, and journal ownership and versions. 2/5 L01 12–18 h

Tier 3 · Grounded Retrieval and Context

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L07 · RAG Document Preparation, Embeddings, and Semantic Search N12, N17, N19 Convert a small textual PDF into traceable chunks with page provenance. Compare a lexical baseline with embeddings appropriate to the application language. 3/5 L03, L06 16–24 h
L08 · RAG Systems, Citations, and Retrieval Improvement N17, N19, N20 Build retrieval → context → generation with verifiable citations. Diagnose failures and justify hybrid search or reranking through comparison. 3/5 L05, L07 18–28 h
L09 · Context Engineering, Memory, and Conversations N04, N19, N22 Budget instructions, evidence, selected journals, history, and memory. Manage memory provenance, correction, deletion, and summarization loss. 3/5 L08 14–22 h

Tier 4 · Production Behavior and Tool Use

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L10 · Concurrency, Streaming, and Long-Running Jobs N02, N18, N25 Separate I/O concurrency from CPU parallelism and avoid blocking the event loop. Handle streaming cancellation, timeouts, and restartable ingestion jobs. 3/5 L08 16–24 h
L11 · Security, Authorization, and Responsible AI N07, N20, N26 Enforce ownership before retrieval, source viewing, history, memory, and job access. Constrain untrusted content, tools, uploads, and consumption. 3/5 L06, L08 16–24 h
L12 · Tool Calling, Bounded Agents, and Multi-Agent Decisions N20, N22, N24 Distinguish fixed workflows from model-directed tool loops. Implement safe tool contracts and evaluate tool/task failures. Understand sub-agent patterns without assuming they improve the product. 4/5 L05, L09, L11 18–28 h
L13 · MCP, Enterprise Integration, and AI Workflows N23, N24 Understand MCP host/client/server roles, primitives, transport, and authorization. Build one bounded integration with explicit failure behavior. 3/5 L12 10–16 h

Tier 5 · Operating and Demonstrating AI Systems

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L14 · MLOps, LLMOps, Architecture, and Deployment N01, N05, N09, N10, N15, N25 Version model/prompt/corpus/evaluation artifacts and trace execution. Release, restore, and diagnose an AI service and its worker. 4/5 L10, L11 20–30 h
L15 · Model Selection, Cost, Latency, and User Feedback N20, N25 Improve a measured bottleneck without sacrificing quality or isolation. Interpret user feedback as a signal rather than unquestioned labels. 4/5 L05, L14 12–18 h
L16 · Capstone Release, Technical Defense, and Career Evidence N27, N28, N29, N30, N31, N32 Demonstrate a usable AI application with evaluation and operating evidence. Explain and modify the implementation independently and communicate honest limitations. 4/5 L02, L03, L08, L09, L10, L11, L12, L13, L14, L15 12–18 h

Tier 6 · Optional Specialization and Supporting UI

Level ITI mapping Focus / output Difficulty Prerequisites Personal time
L17 · Fine-Tuning, Dataset Engineering, and Advanced Serving N12, N15, N21 Choose between prompt changes, RAG, model selection, and fine-tuning using failure evidence. Understand SFT/LoRA, curation, quantization, and serving tradeoffs. 4/5 L02, L03, L05, L15 18–32 h
L18 · Supporting TypeScript and React UI N05, N08 Build a small accessible client for API errors, streaming, and evidence display. Distinguish product UI needs from a complete React/TypeScript curriculum. 2/5 L01, L04, L11 12–20 h

Learning progression

Exact Mermaid diagrams are available in the repository Markdown; prerequisite tables remain readable here.

This compact graph shows the main branches; the Level tables and manifest are the full prerequisite contract. L12 additionally depends on L05; L16 consolidates all core gates.

Pick a sustainable pace

Core path: 233–355 focused hours. Level estimates already include their assigned capstone work; do not add milestone hours again. Optional Levels are separate. Allow contingency for debugging and prior gaps.

Hours/week Core estimate before contingency How to adapt
8–10 23–44 weeks Finish one small gate at a time; keep the corpus and provider count small.
15–20 12–24 weeks Default pace: about 60% implementation, 25% reading, 15% evaluation and notes.
25–30 8–14 weeks Use extra time for tests and failure analysis before adding frameworks.

One capstone, built incrementally

Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant: a FastAPI service that retrieves permitted source passages, cites evidence, connects user-selected journal entries, and manages bounded context. It is a reflection/productivity tool; outputs distinguish source claims from suggestions.

Stage Deliverable Level checkpoint
S0 Scope and Baseline L00
S1 Testable Python API L00, L01
S2 LLM Integration and Scientific Foundations L01, L02, L03, L04
S3 Measured RAG MVP L05, L06, L07, L08
S4 Safe Context-Aware Product L09, L10, L11
S5 Tools, Agents, and MCP L12, L13
S6 Operations and Optimization L14, L15
S7 Portfolio Release and Technical Defense L16

Progress and completion

python scripts/progress.py next
python scripts/progress.py set L00 in_progress
python scripts/progress.py set L00 completed --evidence "evidence/baseline.md"
python scripts/validate.py
python -m unittest discover -s tests -v

Progress instructions explain evidence and waiver requirements. Reading a chapter is not completion: show working behavior, relevant tests, and a decision you can explain without generated code.

Scope decisions

Essential Add only when justified Defer
FastAPI, one model adapter, PostgreSQL/pgvector, evaluation fixtures Hybrid search, reranking, persistent jobs, bounded tools, MCP Multi-agent systems, fine-tuning, GPU optimization, orchestration frameworks.
ML/DL concepts through small CPU labs Extra architecture depth after measured failures Research-level derivations, large training runs, Data Engineering specialization.

Sources, coverage, and limitations

The coverage matrix maps every published syllabus row to Levels and evidence. Representation is not mastery. Optional frontend and fine-tuning material remains visible because ITI names it, while the core path prioritizes practical AI systems. The brochure advertises 678 hours while its listed rows sum to 696; that discrepancy is retained without inventing a correction.

The supplied book contents and relevant sections were checked in the existing research. Sources and pagination explain exact editions, verification boundaries, and resource links. Current provider prices and interview questions are not asserted.

Repository structure and quality

Repository guide · Evaluation · Security · Context Engineering · Career evidence · Learning with AI

Organizational inspiration: Data Engineering Roadmap 2026. Its tier/Level navigation and topic-to-resource approach informed this rebuild; its subjects, hours, level count, and text were not copied.

Start with L00, then build the first tested endpoint in L01.

Start Here

Home · Book reading order · Progress

Your next action

  1. Open L00’s recommended path and run python scripts/progress.py next.
  2. Complete its diagnostic using existing CRM/backend evidence. Record gaps; do not restart beginner Python, REST or SQL courses by default.
  3. Follow the first book slices in the master queue: B01 / Grokking 1, then B02 / Chip 1 planning/stack, then B03 / Chip 4 pipeline preview (skim). The exact page conventions and bounds are there. Stop at each step’s practical task; these are not three whole-book prerequisites to coding.
  4. Record S0 scope evidence, then start L01: one typed endpoint and an HTTP test in a separate application repository.

Choose a stopping point that fits your session. The first useful output is a scope/failure contract; the next is a tested API. These actions need not all fit one evening.

Use each page for one purpose

  • Level README: follow its numbered steps. Use the Primary resource and the named book portion; implement and check the result.
  • resources.md: optional alternatives, official references and advanced material. Choose a replacement when stuck; do not watch the whole library.
  • details.md: diagnostic, technical scope, debugging and self-check answers.
  • Master Book Reading Order: what to read next. Chapter Map: where a section belongs. Later references say Review Only.

Build early: L00 → L01 + L03 → L04 → L05; L06/L07 then support L08. Complete bounded L02 foundations alongside the implementation; they remain required for the core capstone. Use fakes for deterministic tests and budgeted live calls for model evaluations. Waive a Level only with evidence satisfying its gate. Keep application code separate from this roadmap and link your results in the tracker.

ITI Native AI Developer Coverage

Home · Sources · Reading

The official 2026/2027 brochure is five pages. Page 2 publishes prerequisites and678 hours/about five months; pages4–5 list the items below, totaling696 hours. The discrepancy is unresolved. N identifiers are local traceability IDs, not official module numbers.

Official prerequisites: basic/intermediate programming, preferably Python; data structures/algorithms/problem solving; SQL and relational concepts; Git/command line; conceptual statistics/linear algebra and technical English.

Coverage limits and deliberate choices

Topic Treatment
SQL/NoSQL New ORM/session/migration work; existing MongoDB knowledge is assessed rather than repeated
Frontend L18 explicitly covers supporting React/TypeScript; Core needs a basic UI but does not claim the full42-hour React syllabus
Math/ML/DL L02/L03 retain small scientific labs; the three books alone are not a full ML curriculum
Fine-tuning L17 remains visible as optional advanced study; no practical training claim without an actual run
Multi-agent Understand patterns and evaluate a design; implementation is conditional on measured value
MCP L13 includes a small server/client integration using current official documentation
Governance/compliance Threat modeling, privacy boundaries, retention, and responsible behavior; no comprehensive legal/compliance certification claimed
Employability Demonstrations, technical communication, evidence-based CV claims, ethics, and scoped delivery exercises

The mapping is complete traceability, not a claim that self-study equals official training depth or accreditation. Study hours and dependency choices are recommendations.

Industry extensions inside ITI-backed Levels

Recommendation Levels Source boundary
pytest, Pydantic, SQLAlchemy, Alembic L01/L06 Chosen implementations of Python, testing, backend, database topics; not claimed as named ITI mandates.
pgvector, hybrid retrieval, reranking L07/L08 Tool choice and depth added to embeddings/RAG topic coverage.
Durable job semantics, cancellation, CI gates L10/L14 Operational implementation detail recommended beyond the brochure.
OWASP attack fixtures, owner-filtered retrieval, memory deletion L09/L11 Concrete tests and security design supplied by this roadmap.
OpenTelemetry, p95 latency, cost budgets L14/L15 Recommended instrumentation and measurable acceptance evidence.

Master Book Reading Order

Roadmap · Chapter-to-Level map · Progress

Use this page to decide what to read next. Follow the numbered queue below with the Level you are working on. Use the chapter map only to find a section or understand a split. An assignment has one first-reading Level; later appearances are review, not new homework.

Reading status

  • Read Now: first purposeful reading when you reach the stated Level.
  • Read Later: any assignment whose Level/prerequisites you have not reached. Do not front-load it.
  • Skim: read the named ideas selectively; skip familiar setup/theory.
  • Previously Covered / Review Only: look up a specific uncertainty or apply an earlier section; no restart.
  • Optional Advanced: outside the core completion path.

G = Andrea De Mauro, Grokking AI Applications; F = Alireza Parandeh, Building Generative AI Services with FastAPI; C = Chip Huyen, AI Engineering. G/C page numbers are printed pages; F numbers are PDF viewer positions starting at 1. The supplied PDFs remain the verified source; the files are not redistributed.

How the original sequence changed

The baseline was G1–4 → F2–5 → G5–6 → F6–7 → C3–4 → F8–12 → C5–6/C10. The selected chapters remain represented, with these practical changes:

  1. G1 remains early. Selected C1 planning and C4 evaluation-pipeline orientation move to L00 so the project starts with a scope and evidence contract. Neither entire Chip chapter is a prerequisite to coding.
  2. F2/F4 and the pytest slice of F11 move before G2–4 are finished: existing Express experience transfers directly into a small Python API. F3/F5 are split by implementation need rather than read as one block.
  3. C3/C4 evaluation comes before retrieval improvements and agents. G4 and C6 retrieval are read when document search is built, not during a long introductory reading phase.
  4. F7 persistence precedes F6 streaming because ownership, documents and conversation state support RAG. F5 concurrency is expanded when jobs/streaming make it necessary.
  5. Context Engineering and memory precede G5/G6 agent implementation. G5/G6 then follow a working, evaluated RAG service. Fine-tuning and the rest of inference optimization stay optional.
  6. F8/F9 security is applied before tool autonomy; F12 deployment and F10 optimization follow measured behavior. C5/C6/C10 sections are introduced once at their practical decision point and reviewed later only as needed.

Chronological queue

The queue is a sensible default traversal of the prerequisite graph, not a weekly schedule. You can take unlocked branches in a different order; keep the same first-reading/review rules. Only the first new book use in a Level is a reading task; repeated step references mean reuse.

L00 · Baseline Assessment and Product Scope

Open this Level. Before reaching it, its new reading is Read Later.

  1. B01 · G1 — Peeking inside the black box · Read Now

    Printed pp.1–22: LLM/application intuition. Read once; L03 reviews it.

    Purpose: Start with the accessible introduction from the original plan.

  1. B02 · C1 — Introduction · Read Now

    Printed pp.28–34: Planning AI Applications; pp.35–46: The AI Engineering Stack. Skim history.

    Purpose: Adds product scope before implementation, without reading all of C1.

  1. B03 · C4 — Evaluate AI Systems · Skim

    Printed pp.200–207: Design Your Evaluation Pipeline. Preview the workflow; L05 applies it.

    Purpose: Define evidence before the demo grows.

L01 · Python, FastAPI, and Service Testing

Open this Level. Before reaching it, its new reading is Read Later.

  1. B04 · F2 — Getting Started with FastAPI · Read Now

    PDF pp.43–82: server setup, Depends, lifespan and project boundaries. Skim familiar HTTP/framework comparisons.

    Purpose: Build a tested endpoint before finishing the conceptual books.

  1. B05 · F4 — Implementing Type-Safe AI Services · Read Now

    PDF pp.153–186: annotations, Annotated, Pydantic constraints/serialization/settings. Skim primitive Python types.

    Purpose: Python-specific validation is a genuine transfer gap.

  1. B06 · F11 — Testing AI Services · Read Now

    PDF pp.501–518: unit tests, pytest fixtures/parametrization, async tests and doubles. Other Chapter 11 sections are later assignments.

    Purpose: Move only the needed testing portion forward.

L02 · Practical Mathematics, ML, and Data Preparation

Open this Level. Before reaching it, its new reading is Read Later.

  1. B07 · C8 — Dataset Engineering · Skim

    Printed pp.368–371: data quality. Use as motivation; scikit-learn teaches the actual foundation lab.

    Purpose: A short quality lens, not a new data-science curriculum.

L03 · Deep Learning, Foundation Models, and Serving

Open this Level. Before reaching it, its new reading is Read Later.

  1. B08 · C2 — Understanding Foundation Models · Read Now

    Printed pp.49–87: model architecture/training orientation; pp.88–105: sampling and structured-output concepts. Avoid research derivations.

    Purpose: Understand the model interface and behavior before relying on it.

  1. B09 · F3 — AI Integration and Model Serving · Read Now

    PDF pp.83–105: language-model integration; pp.137–142: serving/lifespan boundaries. Defer other modalities.

    Purpose: Separate inference integration from training.

Previously Covered / Review Only: B01. Consult only for the current implementation/failure; do not read those chapters again.

L04 · Prompt Engineering, Structured Outputs, and LLM Integration

Open this Level. Before reaching it, its new reading is Read Later.

  1. B10 · G2 — Building your first GenAI app · Read Now

Printed pp.42–45: What if we coded it? Skim pp.23–41 only for orientation; no Langflow setup requirement.

Purpose: Preserves the original introduction while avoiding low-code detours.

  1. B11 · G3 — Writing the right prompt · Read Now

Printed pp.48–56 and 68–72: prompt techniques and their implementation. Skim repeated UI examples.

Purpose: Primary prompt explanation; C5 supplies only missing engineering details.

  1. B14 · C5 — Prompt Engineering · Skim

Printed pp.218–234: context efficiency, prompt construction/versioning. Skim prompt techniques already learned in B11.

Purpose: First pass includes context basics; L09 revisits pp.218–219 rather than reassigning them.

  1. B12 · F3 — AI Integration and Model Serving · Read Now

PDF pp.142–147: external serving/API boundary. The overlap page 142 is a section transition, not a chapter restart.

Purpose: Later slice of F3 after its serving concepts in B09.

  1. B13 · F5 — Achieving Concurrency in AI Workloads · Read Now

PDF pp.211–212: async handler/provider call example. Use current HTTP client docs; full concurrency theory is B31.

Purpose: The provider API example is here; pp.203–208 teach async concepts rather than a separate provider chapter.

Previously Covered / Review Only: B05, B08. Consult only for the current implementation/failure; do not read those chapters again.

L05 · Evaluation Engineering

Open this Level. Before reaching it, its new reading is Read Later.

  1. B16 · C4 — Evaluate AI Systems · Read Now

Printed pp.160–178: task criteria and operational criteria. pp.200–207 were previewed in B03; review/apply, do not restart.

Purpose: Model-selection pp.179–200 remain B40.

  1. B15 · C3 — Evaluation Methodology · Read Now

Printed pp.114–117 and 125–147: challenges, exact measures, similarity and AI judges. Defer entropy derivations.

Purpose: Evaluation moves before agents and retrieval optimization.

  1. B17 · F11 — Testing AI Services · Read Now

PDF pp.495–500: model-testing challenges; pp.523–528: behavioral tests. Reuse pytest knowledge from B06.

Purpose: Different sections from the first pytest pass; retrieval/E2E are B26/B38.

Previously Covered / Review Only: B03, B06. Consult only for the current implementation/failure; do not read those chapters again.

L06 · PostgreSQL, ORM, and AI Application State

Open this Level. Before reaching it, its new reading is Read Later.

  1. B18 · F7 — Integrating Databases into AI Services · Read Now

PDF pp.302–337: SQLAlchemy models, engine/session, CRUD, Alembic. Skim DB comparisons, known SQL and Prisma.

Purpose: Persistence precedes RAG and durable state, so F7 comes before F6.

L07 · RAG Document Preparation, Embeddings, and Semantic Search

Open this Level. Before reaching it, its new reading is Read Later.

  1. B21 · F5 — Achieving Concurrency in AI Workloads · Read Now

Why chapters are split

  • F3: model/serving orientation in B09; external API integration in B12. Other modalities remain optional.
  • F5: provider-call example B13; preparation B21; RAG generation B25; concurrency/jobs B31; advanced inference is optional B47. Verified examples corrected the earlier broad provider/RAG page descriptions.
  • F11: pytest B06 (PDF501–518); model/behavioral testing B17 (495–500, 523–528); retrieval integration B26 (519–522); E2E B38 (529–534). Later agent/release work reuses these skills.
  • C4: pipeline preview B03, task criteria B16, model selection B40. The preview is applied in L05, not reintroduced as a whole new chapter.
  • C5: prompt/context basics B14, defense B35. L09 context-budget reading is explicit review B30.
  • C6: retrieval foundations B20, improvements B27, memory B28, agents B36b. One chapter supports different project capabilities without a mandatory full reread.
  • C8/C9: bounded quality/processing/metrics slices enter the core; optional training/serving sections wait for L17.
  • C10: context B29, guardrails B35a, orchestration B37, observability B39a, feedback B43. The old pp.465–473 assignment is split to avoid reassigning orchestration at L14.

Retained topic associations

The original topic-to-book research is retained in each topic’s book field in RESOURCE_CATALOG.json and the manifest’s reading_topic_notes. These fields explain conceptual overlap; they do not introduce new assignments. The reading ledger is authoritative.

Book-Grounded Reflection Assistant

Home · Architecture · Evaluation

Implement the application yourself in a separate repository. Link its commits, tests, reports, and demos as evidence here. This package is a learning repository, not a finished product.

Milestone Required modules Delivery
S0 L00 Scope and Baseline
S1 L00, L01 Testable Python API
S2 L01, L02, L03, L04 LLM Integration and Scientific Foundations
S3 L05, L06, L07, L08 Measured RAG MVP
S4 L09, L10, L11 Safe Context-Aware Product
S5 L12, L13 Tools, Agents, and MCP
S6 L14, L15 Operations and Optimization
S7 L16 Portfolio Release and Technical Defense
Product behavior Acceptance direction
Upload Start with a small permitted text PDF; record parsing failures and provenance
Ask Answer from authorized relevant passages and show verifiable citations
Reflect Connect a user-selected journal entry with source ideas; distinguish interpretation from source claims
Continue Maintain bounded history and editable, sourced memory
Act Use bounded authorized tools only when justified
Operate Evaluate, trace, recover, and report operating limits

S3 is the MVP; S4 is intermediate; S5–S7 add integration and production-oriented evidence. S5 and S6 may proceed independently after prerequisites. L17 and broader multi-agent implementations are optional. The capstone requires a basic UI; React specifically is the L18 option, not a Core blocker.

Initial Architecture Decisions

Project · ADR template

Component Initial choice Reason
API FastAPI and Pydantic Transfer backend knowledge with typed contracts
State PostgreSQL, SQLAlchemy, Alembic Relations, ownership, transactions, and migrations
Retrieval pgvector One database to start; exact retrieval before measured ANN need
Models One direct-SDK provider adapter Keep integration inspectable and testable
Files Private local storage, then persistent volume/object storage Preserve source access and deployment state
Jobs One worker/queue when necessary Isolate long-running work and test retry semantics
Architecture Modular monolith Avoid premature service boundaries

Exact Mermaid diagrams are available in the repository Markdown; prerequisite tables remain readable here.

Contract Required behavior
Authorization Scope retrieval and source access before content enters context
Citations Model references come from a whitelist; the server validates IDs and displays source metadata
Ownership The model cannot choose an authoritative user_id or execute arbitrary SQL
Provenance Save document/page/offset/hash/chunker/model/dimension/version/status
Sessions API and concurrent worker tasks do not share one mutable session
Memory Preserve source/date/owner and support edit/delete
Search Exact nearest-neighbor search is not a guarantee of relevant evidence; model dimensions are not guessed from generation-model hidden size

Initial entities: users, documents, document_versions, chunks, ingestion_runs, conversations, messages, and journal_entries. Record actual alternatives in ADRs rather than treating these recommendations as official ITI tools.

L00 · Baseline Assessment and Product Scope

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 1 · 3–5 focused hours · Difficulty 1/5 · P0

You will learn: Identify transferable backend skills and specific gaps. Define users, requirements, failure cases, and acceptance evidence.

Before starting: Use your existing backend, Python and SQL experience. Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B01 · G1 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Transfer backend skills

Learn: Express handlers → FastAPI routes; Zod → Pydantic; Jest → pytest; Mongoose → ORM boundaries.

Required reading:

  • B01 · G1 — Read Now. Printed pp.1–22: LLM/application intuition. Read once; L03 reviews it.
  • B02 · C1 — Read Now. Printed pp.28–34: Planning AI Applications; pp.35–46: The AI Engineering Stack. Skim history.

Primary · Single video + companion repository: Pythonation FastAPI tutorial and companion code. Consult the Express handlers → FastAPI routes; Zod → Pydantic; Jest → pytest; Mongoose → ORM boundaries sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Record a comparison with one existing CRM implementation and one Python gap.

Completion check: Name one working CRM artifact and one specific Python gap.

Alternative · AR video + assignments (optional) · Implementation reference

2. Choose the user problem

Learn: Book questions; journal connections; non-goals; permitted document corpus.

Required reading:

  • B02 · C1 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · What will we build ماذا سنبنى في المشروع. Only the linked “What will we build ماذا سنبنى في المشروع” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Write five user stories with acceptance conditions and only one first-release flow.

Completion check: Five stories, three non-goals and a single first-release flow exist.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Define answer contracts

Learn: Evidence versus suggestion; no-evidence behavior; ownership.

Required reading:

  • B03 · C4 — Skim. Printed pp.200–207: Design Your Evaluation Pipeline. Preview the workflow; L05 applies it.

Primary · Machine-translated lesson / article: AI user experience · Arabic. Only the linked translated lesson, particularly its sections about Define answer contracts. Its English video/cloud build is an alternative, not an extra requirement.

Practice: Write three response examples: supported, unsupported and unauthorized.

Completion check: Supported, unsupported and unauthorized responses are visibly different.

Alternative · EN video + slides (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Plan evaluation evidence

Learn: Answerable/unanswerable questions; expected source; fixed synthetic cases.

Required reading:

  • B03 · C4 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Single lecture video + slides: LLMOps. Only the linked “LLMOps” lecture; not the complete LLM Bootcamp. Use the parts relevant to Plan evaluation evidence. Exact timestamps are not assigned.

Practice: Create ten initial questions with source references; label these development cases.

Completion check: Ten cases have evidence labels and a stated pass/fail criterion.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Learn independently

Learn: Read → recall → implement → test → explain; AI assistance boundaries.

Required book reading: None for this step. The focused tutorial fills a gap not taught by the three books.

Primary · Course unit: lecture + problem set: CS50P · Unit Tests. Week 5 “Unit Tests”: assertions/pytest explanation only; use it to calibrate the diagnostic.

Practice: Explain the use case in two minutes and implement a tiny change without AI.

Completion check: Explain the product in two minutes and implement a small independent change.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Record demonstrated skills and gaps; distinguish prior evidence from assumptions.
    • Pass: Each story has an observable acceptance condition.
    • Pass: A one-page scope and ten initial questions are sufficient to start building.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S0. Explain the use case and the first deliverable in two minutes. Review only demonstrated gaps rather than restarting backend fundamentals.

Keep: baseline.md with links to prior work; project-scope.md and the first ADR.

What to defer

  • Repeating JavaScript, HTTP, Git, or Docker basics after passing diagnostics
  • Microservices or buying a GPU before a need exists

Record progress and choose the next Level

python scripts/progress.py set L00 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

Unlocks: L01, L02, L03

Resource Library · L00 — Baseline Assessment and Product Scope

← Follow the learning path · Technical details · Master book order

This is the complete retained library, not a second to-do list. Follow Primary resources in the README; everything else is an Alternative, Reference or Optional Advanced resource. You do not need to watch every video or finish every course.

Selection key

  • Primary: used by at least one numbered step; follow only its assigned portion.
  • Alternative: optional Arabic/English explanation or practice replacing a primary explanation.
  • Reference: consult a named API/spec section when implementing.
  • Optional Advanced: a later deep dive; does not block this Level.

Resource Type describes the format, independently of its role. A single video containing a playlist parameter remains a single video. Exact titles/numbers are used where verified; unverified identifiers are explicitly marked.

Book references

Assigned reading is authoritative in the master order for L00. The chapter map preserves full chapter/page locations and split reasons. This library does not assign those chapters again.

This Level’s ledger IDs: B01 · G1, B02 · C1, B03 · C4.

Technical Details · L00

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Backend transfer: Explain validation, auth, persistence and one integration test from CRM.
  • Python/SQL baseline: Write a JSON transformation and a small join without generated code.
  • Product reasoning: Name a user, their question, a failure and observable success.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Transfer backend skills: Express handlers → FastAPI routes; Zod → Pydantic; Jest → pytest; Mongoose → ORM boundaries.
  • Choose the user problem: Book questions; journal connections; non-goals; permitted document corpus.
  • Define answer contracts: Evidence versus suggestion; no-evidence behavior; ownership.
  • Plan evaluation evidence: Answerable/unanswerable questions; expected source; fixed synthetic cases.
  • Learn independently: Read → recall → implement → test → explain; AI assistance boundaries.

Common mistakes and debugging

Planning everything

Freeze three non-goals and build the first endpoint next.

Vague AI success

Turn “helpful answer” into groundedness, citation and abstention criteria.

Copying a tutorial product

Use your own reflection/journal user stories and corpus.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. What makes the first release small?
  2. What counts as prior skill evidence?
  3. Why draft evaluation questions now?
Answer guide

What makes the first release small?

One user flow, a small corpus, explicit non-goals and observable acceptance criteria.

What counts as prior skill evidence?

A specific route, test, schema or commit you can explain and modify.

Why draft evaluation questions now?

They expose ambiguous scope and establish a baseline before optimization.

  • N31: Work Ethics Workshop — official published module title.

Learning objectives and evidence

L01 · Python, FastAPI, and Service Testing

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 1 · 14–20 focused hours · Difficulty 2/5 · P0

You will learn: Use type hints, Pydantic, dependency injection, and lifespan management. Separate HTTP handlers from application logic and test the service.

Before starting: L00 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B04 · F2 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Routes and application boundaries

Learn: APIRouter; config; handlers versus services; fake provider.

Required reading:

  • B04 · F2 — Read Now. PDF pp.43–82: server setup, Depends, lifespan and project boundaries. Skim familiar HTTP/framework comparisons.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · Nested Routes + Env Values. Only the linked “Nested Routes + Env Values” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Create /health and /reflect with application logic outside the route handler.

Completion check: A clean checkout serves /health and /reflect with a fake provider.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

2. Dependency injection and lifecycle

Learn: Depends; dependency overrides; shared HTTP client; lifespan teardown.

Required reading:

  • B04 · F2 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: FastAPI dependencies. Read FastAPI’s Dependencies introduction and dependency overrides; follow its lifespan link for startup/teardown. Implement shared-client lifetime yourself using those primitives.

Practice: Swap providers in a test and assert the lifespan client closes after shutdown.

Completion check: An override swaps providers and shutdown closes the shared client.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Modern Python tooling and types

Learn: Environment; dependency pinning; type hints; Annotated; optional versus nullable.

Required reading:

  • B05 · F4 — Read Now. PDF pp.153–186: annotations, Annotated, Pydantic constraints/serialization/settings. Skim primitive Python types.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Python typing. Read typing’s Annotated, Optional and basic collection annotations; use your existing environment setup. This page explains typing, not dependency management.

Practice: Type a provider interface and explain what is checked statically versus at runtime.

Completion check: Explain why annotation alone does not reject an invalid value.

Alternative · AR video + assignments (optional)

4. Request and response contracts

Learn: Pydantic validation; serialization; constraints; strictness; errors.

Required reading:

  • B05 · F4 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Pydantic models. Consult the Pydantic validation; serialization; constraints; strictness; errors sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Reject missing, malformed and unexpected values with an explicit response contract.

Completion check: Malformed and missing fields return documented errors.

Alternative · AR video + code (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Iteration and resource cleanup

Learn: Generators; batching; with; context managers; exceptions.

Required book reading: None for this step. The focused tutorial fills a gap not taught by the three books.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Context managers. Read contextmanager/asynccontextmanager and closing/aclosing examples. Review Python generators only if the diagnostic shows a gap; batching is your exercise, not a contextlib API.

Practice: Batch a record iterator and prove cleanup when its consumer raises.

Completion check: An exception still runs cleanup; batching loses no records.

Alternative · AR video + assignments (optional)

6. Deterministic service tests

Learn: Fixtures; parametrization; mocks; API contracts; error branches.

Required reading:

  • B06 · F11 — Read Now. PDF pp.501–518: unit tests, pytest fixtures/parametrization, async tests and doubles. Other Chapter 11 sections are later assignments.

Primary · Course unit: lecture + problem set: CS50P · Unit Tests. Week 5 “Unit Tests”: lecture sections on assert/pytest and associated problem-set tasks for your diagnostic gaps.

Practice: Run validation, provider-error and dependency-override tests with no external calls.

Completion check: Tests cover validation, provider errors and an override without live calls.

Alternative · AR video + assignments (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Tests make no live model calls and assert documented responses.
    • Pass: An exception triggers cleanup; batches preserve the expected records.
    • Pass: The API contract stays the same when fake and real adapters are swapped.
    • Pass: Demonstrate the distinction with a failing validation case; deepen concurrency in L10.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S1. A clean checkout runs the API and tests. Provider failures have defined responses, and you can add and test a small feature independently.

Keep: Application skeleton in a separate implementation repository; Tests, pinned dependencies, and setup instructions.

What to defer

  • A complete Python beginner course if diagnostics pass
  • Agent frameworks and elaborate class hierarchies

Record progress and choose the next Level

python scripts/progress.py set L01 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

Unlocks: L04, L06, L18

Resource Library · L01 — Python, FastAPI, and Service Testing

This is the complete retained library, not a second to-do list. Follow Primary resources in the README; everything else is an Alternative, Reference or Optional Advanced resource. You do not need to watch every video or finish every course.

Selection key

  • Primary: used by at least one numbered step; follow only its assigned portion.
  • Alternative: optional Arabic/English explanation or practice replacing a primary explanation.
  • Reference: consult a named API/spec section when implementing.
  • Optional Advanced: a later deep dive; does not block this Level.

Resource Type describes the format, independently of its role. A single video containing a playlist parameter remains a single video. Exact titles/numbers are used where verified; unverified identifiers are explicitly marked.

Assigned reading is authoritative in the master order for L01. The chapter map preserves full chapter/page locations and split reasons. This library does not assign those chapters again.

Technical Details · L01

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Python transfer: Use functions, OOP, exceptions, modules and virtual environments without a beginner walkthrough.
  • HTTP transfer: Explain status codes, validation and middleware from Express.
  • Testing transfer: Write one success and one failure assertion independently.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Routes and application boundaries: APIRouter; config; handlers versus services; fake provider.
  • Dependency injection and lifecycle: Depends; dependency overrides; shared HTTP client; lifespan teardown.
  • Modern Python tooling and types: Environment; dependency pinning; type hints; Annotated; optional versus nullable.
  • Request and response contracts: Pydantic validation; serialization; constraints; strictness; errors.
  • Iteration and resource cleanup: Generators; batching; with; context managers; exceptions.
  • Deterministic service tests: Fixtures; parametrization; mocks; API contracts; error branches.

Common mistakes and debugging

Annotations treated as validation

Show a plain typed function accepting a bad value and a Pydantic model rejecting it.

One global mutable client/session

Initialize shared clients in lifespan; use request/task-specific state.

Tests call a paid model

Override the provider and make tests deterministic.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. Why Depends rather than manual construction in every route?
  2. Does async make CPU work faster?
  3. What must cleanup prove?
Answer guide

Why Depends rather than manual construction in every route?

It provides a declared lifecycle and an override seam while keeping application logic testable.

Does async make CPU work faster?

No; it enables cooperative I/O concurrency. CPU work needs a different execution boundary.

What must cleanup prove?

Resources close on both success and exceptions; tests verify the teardown.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N02: Advanced Python Programming for AI Engineers — official published module title.
  • N05: Software Testing & Full-Stack Debugging — official published module title.
  • N07: RESTful API, Backend Architecture & Security — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

L02 · Practical Mathematics, ML, and Data Preparation

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 1 · 16–24 focused hours · Difficulty 2/5 · P1

You will learn: Understand vectors, matrices, similarity, probability, loss, and gradients through small examples. Run an ML baseline with correct splits, preprocessing, and evaluation. Recognize leakage and distinguish a similarity score from a calibrated probability.

Before starting: L00 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Vectors and matrix intuition

Learn: Shapes; dot product; norms; cosine; matrix multiplication.

Required book reading: None for this step. The focused tutorial fills a gap not taught by the three books.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: NumPy beginners guide. Consult the Shapes; dot product; norms; cosine; matrix multiplication sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Calculate a two-vector cosine by hand and match NumPy within tolerance.

Completion check: Hand and NumPy cosine values agree within numeric tolerance.

Alternative · AR video (optional)

2. Probability and uncertainty

Learn: Conditional probability; expectation; noisy measurements; sample size.

Required book reading: None for this step. The focused tutorial fills a gap not taught by the three books.

Primary · Modular course: videos + interactive exercises: Machine Learning Crash Course. Logistic Regression probability interpretation and its Check Your Understanding exercise only.

Practice: Explain a conditional-probability example and report raw counts beside a rate.

Completion check: Report raw counts and explain why a small measured rate is uncertain.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Loss and gradient intuition

Learn: Loss versus metric; slope; learning rate; optimization.

Required book reading: None for this step. The focused tutorial fills a gap not taught by the three books.

Primary · Modular course: videos + interactive exercises: Machine Learning Crash Course. Linear Regression: loss and gradient-descent sections only.

Practice: Take one manual gradient step on a scalar loss and show whether it improved.

Completion check: A manual gradient step changes the scalar loss in the expected direction.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Preparation and split discipline

Learn: Missing values; scaling; train/validation/test; grouped/temporal leakage.

Required reading:

  • B07 · C8 — Skim. Printed pp.368–371: data quality. Use as motivation; scikit-learn teaches the actual foundation lab.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Common ML pitfalls. Consult the Missing values; scaling; train/validation/test; grouped/temporal leakage sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Fit preprocessing on train only; create a deliberately leaky comparison and explain it.

Completion check: The test set never participates in fitting preprocessing.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

5. ML families and baselines

Learn: Regression; classification; clustering intuition; overfitting; regularization.

Required book reading: None for this step. The focused tutorial fills a gap not taught by the three books.

Primary · Modular course: videos + interactive exercises: Machine Learning Crash Course. Regression/classification overview plus Datasets/Overfitting sections needed for the baseline; no entire-course replay.

Practice: Compare one simple classifier with a dummy baseline and describe train/test behavior.

Completion check: A dummy baseline and a fitted classifier are compared on held-out data.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Metrics for the task

Learn: Confusion matrix; precision/recall; F1; MAE; ranking is a later task.

Required book reading: None for this step. The focused tutorial fills a gap not taught by the three books.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Classification and regression metrics. Consult the Confusion matrix; precision/recall; F1; MAE; ranking is a later task sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Choose metrics for an imbalanced classifier and explain an error-cost tradeoff.

Completion check: Explain the confusion matrix and why the chosen metric fits error costs.

Alternative · AR video (optional)

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Check orthogonal-vector similarity, identical-vector similarity, and the zero-vector policy.
    • Pass: Show denominators and handling of undefined cases; explain what each metric misses.
    • Pass: Fit preprocessing only on training data; save split, seed, and metrics.
    • Pass: Identify the leaked information and reproduce both configurations; no target score is required.
    • Pass: Describe the direction of an update and the difference between training and generalization.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S2. Compute simple similarity and classification metrics, explain train/validation/test, and reproduce the ML experiment without leaking test information.

Keep: math-notes.md and a small reproducible ML script or notebook; Leakage comparison with data source or generation seed.

What to defer

  • A full Data Science specialization
  • Advanced proofs or prolonged competition tuning

Record progress and choose the next Level

python scripts/progress.py set L02 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

Unlocks: L16, L17

Technical Details · L02

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Math baseline: Manipulate arrays and explain a mean, probability and function slope.
  • Python prerequisite: Write functions and load a small tabular dataset.
  • Application link: Explain why retrieval similarity and predictive confidence are different.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Vectors and matrix intuition: Shapes; dot product; norms; cosine; matrix multiplication.
  • Probability and uncertainty: Conditional probability; expectation; noisy measurements; sample size.
  • Loss and gradient intuition: Loss versus metric; slope; learning rate; optimization.
  • Preparation and split discipline: Missing values; scaling; train/validation/test; grouped/temporal leakage.
  • ML families and baselines: Regression; classification; clustering intuition; overfitting; regularization.
  • Metrics for the task: Confusion matrix; precision/recall; F1; MAE; ranking is a later task.

Common mistakes and debugging

Leakage during preprocessing

Fit transforms only on training data and prove it in a pipeline.

Accuracy on an imbalanced set

Compare to a trivial baseline and inspect precision/recall.

Turning this into a long DS track

Stop after one correct baseline and a short explanation of uncertainty.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. What is data leakage?
  2. Why normalize vectors?
  3. Is cosine 0.8 an 80% probability?
Answer guide

What is data leakage?

Training or selection uses information unavailable at prediction time, including test-set preprocessing.

Why normalize vectors?

It changes similarity geometry; cosine compares direction after accounting for magnitude.

Is cosine 0.8 an 80% probability?

No. It is a geometric score, not a calibrated event probability.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N11: Mathematical Foundations — official published module title.
  • N12: Data Preparation & Feature Engineering — official published module title.
  • N13: Machine Learning Fundamentals — official published module title.

L03 · Deep Learning, Foundation Models, and Serving

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 1 · 12–18 focused hours · Difficulty 3/5 · P0

You will learn: Distinguish pretraining, supervised fine-tuning, preference tuning, and inference. Understand layers, autograd, attention, tokenization, sampling, and resource constraints. Choose an external provider or local inference endpoint with a reasoned boundary.

Before starting: L00 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B08 · C2 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Tensors and neural network structure

Learn: Layers; activations; shapes; device; dtype.

Required reading:

  • B08 · C2 — Read Now. Printed pp.49–87: model architecture/training orientation; pp.88–105: sampling and structured-output concepts. Avoid research derivations.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Tensors. Consult the Layers; activations; shapes; device; dtype sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Run a small forward pass and assert each intermediate shape.

Completion check: Shapes, dtype and device are asserted for the tiny forward pass.

Alternative · AR video (optional)

2. Backpropagation and optimization

Learn: Autograd; computational graph; gradients; train versus eval.

Required reading:

  • B08 · C2 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Autograd. Consult the Autograd; computational graph; gradients; train versus eval sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Check a scalar derivative manually and train a small CPU network for a few steps.

Completion check: Manual derivative matches autograd and the tiny model loss changes.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Language-model foundations

Learn: Next-token prediction; pretraining; SFT; preference tuning.

Required reading:

  • B01 · G1 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B08 · C2 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Full course: text, videos and quizzes: Hugging Face LLM course. Chapter 1: Transformer families, how Transformers work, inference and bias/limitations. Tokenizers are the separate chapter-6 reference.

Practice: Explain where knowledge, instruction behavior and application context enter the system.

Completion check: Explain pretraining, SFT, preference tuning and inference using the same model boundary.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Tokens, context and sampling

Learn: Tokenizer; context limit; output length; temperature; top-p.

Required reading:

  • B08 · C2 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Tokenizers. Chapter 6 introduction to tokenization; use the tokenizer/model example needed for the Arabic/English count experiment. No whole-course assignment.

Practice: Measure token counts for Arabic/English prompts and compare two sampling settings.

Completion check: Record model/config and compare actual token counts and sampling outputs.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Attention and transformer intuition

Learn: Queries/keys/values; attention weights; positional information.

Required reading:

  • B08 · C2 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Single video + illustrated lesson: Neural networks visual series. Only “Attention in transformers, step-by-step”. The general series is an optional alternative, not an assignment to watch every lesson.

Practice: Explain attention using a short sentence and trace tensor dimensions conceptually.

Completion check: Explain Q/K/V and the dimensions without claiming to have trained a transformer.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Training versus model serving

Learn: API provider; local server; model license; memory/latency budget.

Required reading:

  • B09 · F3 — Read Now. PDF pp.83–105: language-model integration; pp.137–142: serving/lifespan boundaries. Defer other modalities.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · Ollama Local LLM Server. Only the linked “Ollama Local LLM Server” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Call one local or hosted inference endpoint behind an adapter and record the resource boundary.

Completion check: The API calls an external/local inference adapter with documented failure behavior.

Alternative · EN video + slides (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Record tensor shapes, inspect gradients, and explain the update. Use L02 exercise E1 first if vectors are unfamiliar.
    • Pass: Distinguish characters, tokens, context capacity, and embedding dimensions.
    • Pass: Record settings and variation; low temperature is not treated as a determinism guarantee.
    • Pass: Document timeouts, lifecycle, privacy/resource constraints, and actual hardware assumptions.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S2. Explain next-token prediction and attention conceptually, demonstrate the autograd lab, and run inference. L02 may proceed in parallel; completing all ML topics is not a prerequisite for the first model API call.

Keep: tiny-autograd.py and tokenization observations; Model selection card and serving ADR.

What to defer

  • Training a transformer from scratch
  • Audio/video/3D serving and distributed GPU training

python scripts/progress.py set L03 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

Technical Details · L03

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Math readiness (review L02 if needed): Explain a loss, gradient, train/test split and vector shape.
  • Python readiness: Use a small tensor/array and inspect dimensions.
  • System boundary: Separate a web server process from a model-serving process.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Tensors and neural network structure: Layers; activations; shapes; device; dtype.
  • Backpropagation and optimization: Autograd; computational graph; gradients; train versus eval.
  • Language-model foundations: Next-token prediction; pretraining; SFT; preference tuning.
  • Tokens, context and sampling: Tokenizer; context limit; output length; temperature; top-p.
  • Attention and transformer intuition: Queries/keys/values; attention weights; positional information.
  • Training versus model serving: API provider; local server; model license; memory/latency budget.

Common mistakes and debugging

Skipping shapes

Print shape/dtype/device at each step and assert expected dimensions.

Assuming sampling is fully deterministic

Record model/version/config and repeat outputs; temperature zero is not a universal reproducibility guarantee.

Training before integration

Use a CPU toy model for understanding; integrate an existing model for the app.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. What does backpropagation compute?
  2. Why do token counts differ in Arabic and English?
  3. What is post-training versus inference?
Answer guide

What does backpropagation compute?

Derivatives of a loss with respect to parameters using the chain rule.

Why do token counts differ in Arabic and English?

The tokenizer vocabulary and segmentation differ; measure the actual model tokenizer.

What is post-training versus inference?

Post-training updates parameters or preferences; inference uses the selected parameters to produce outputs.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N14: Deep Learning & Neural Networks — official published module title.
  • N15: MLOps Basics & Model Serving — official published module title.
  • N16: LLM Fundamentals & GenAI Landscape — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

The assessed exercise criteria are in the guided page. For evidence use EVIDENCE and EXPERIMENT.

L04 · Prompt Engineering, Structured Outputs, and LLM Integration

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 2 · 10–16 focused hours · Difficulty 2/5 · P0

You will learn: Design instructions, examples, schemas, and untrusted-input boundaries. Integrate one model behind an adapter with controlled error handling.

Before starting: L01, L03 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B10 · G2 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Instructions and examples

Learn: Task; role; delimiters; few-shot examples; output constraints.

Required reading:

  • B10 · G2 — Read Now. Printed pp.42–45: What if we coded it? Skim pp.23–41 only for orientation; no Langflow setup requirement.
  • B11 · G3 — Read Now. Printed pp.48–56 and 68–72: prompt techniques and their implementation. Skim repeated UI examples.

Primary · Interactive tutorial / notebook exercises: Interactive prompt engineering tutorial. Consult the Task; role; delimiters; few-shot examples; output constraints sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Compare a baseline and revised prompt on ten fixed examples.

Completion check: The revised prompt is compared on the same ten cases as the baseline.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

2. Prompt lifecycle

Learn: Template variables; version control; config; regression cases.

Required reading:

  • B14 · C5 — Skim. Printed pp.218–234: context efficiency, prompt construction/versioning. Skim prompt techniques already learned in B11.

Primary · Documentation: assertions reference: Assertions and metrics. The assertion type matching Prompt lifecycle; start with deterministic checks, then calibrated model grading if needed. Do not adopt all plugins.

Practice: Store prompt v1/v2 and record the exact configuration and changed failures.

Completion check: Prompt versions and changed failure cases are recorded.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

3. Provider integration

Learn: SDK/HTTP boundary; errors; credentials via environment; limits.

Required reading:

  • B12 · F3 — Read Now. PDF pp.142–147: external serving/API boundary. The overlap page 142 is a section transition, not a chapter restart.
  • B13 · F5 — Read Now. PDF pp.211–212: async handler/provider call example. Use current HTTP client docs; full concurrency theory is B31.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · LLM Factory. Only the linked “LLM Factory” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Implement a real adapter with connect/read timeout and a fake equivalent.

Completion check: A fake and real adapter implement the same timeout/error contract.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Structured output validation

Learn: Schema; enum/constraints; invalid JSON; refusal; repair boundary.

Required reading:

  • B05 · F4 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B08 · C2 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Ollama structured outputs. Consult the Schema; enum/constraints; invalid JSON; refusal; repair boundary sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Reject invalid schema output and distinguish provider refusal from parse failure.

Completion check: Invalid schema output is rejected; schema validity is not reported as factual correctness.

Alternative · AR video + code (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Untrusted input boundaries

Learn: Document text versus instructions; evidence labels; tool output trust.

Required reading:

  • B14 · C5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: LLM prompt injection. Consult the Document text versus instructions; evidence labels; tool output trust sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Test a journal entry that tries to override system behavior and record the failure.

Completion check: A hostile journal entry cannot change backend permissions.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

6. Small AI endpoint

Learn: One call; request IDs; output contract; no low-code dependency.

Required reading:

  • B10 · G2 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · Welcome to FastAPI. Only the linked “Welcome to FastAPI” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Ship /reflect with explicit error behavior and a reproducible curl example.

Completion check: The endpoint has an independent test and a reproducible example request.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Validate the response schema on the server and reject unknown reference IDs.
    • Pass: Separate schema failure, factual failure, and transport failure.
    • Pass: Save prompt/model versions, usage, latency, and the observed differences.
    • Pass: Permanent failures do not loop; time and attempt limits are tested.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S2. No secrets are committed. Valid JSON is not mistaken for a correct answer. The same application logic works with fake and real providers.

Keep: Provider adapter, versioned prompt, and structured-output tests; Small prompt comparison report.

What to defer

  • Many providers at once
  • Prompt frameworks or autonomous agent loops

Record progress and choose the next Level

python scripts/progress.py set L04 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

Unlocks: L05, L18

Resource Library · L04 — Prompt Engineering, Structured Outputs, and LLM Integration

← Follow the learning path · Technical details · Master book order

This is the complete retained library, not a second to-do list. Follow Primary resources in the README; everything else is an Alternative, Reference or Optional Advanced resource. You do not need to watch every video or finish every course.

Selection key

  • Primary: used by at least one numbered step; follow only its assigned portion.
  • Alternative: optional Arabic/English explanation or practice replacing a primary explanation.
  • Reference: consult a named API/spec section when implementing.
  • Optional Advanced: a later deep dive; does not block this Level.

Resource Type describes the format, independently of its role. A single video containing a playlist parameter remains a single video. Exact titles/numbers are used where verified; unverified identifiers are explicitly marked.

Book references

Assigned reading is authoritative in the master order for L04. The chapter map preserves full chapter/page locations and split reasons. This library does not assign those chapters again.

Technical Details · L04

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • L01 readiness: A fake provider can be replaced through a tested dependency.
  • Model readiness: Explain tokens, sampling and context limits.
  • Contract readiness: Specify expected response fields and provider error behavior.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Instructions and examples: Task; role; delimiters; few-shot examples; output constraints.
  • Prompt lifecycle: Template variables; version control; config; regression cases.
  • Provider integration: SDK/HTTP boundary; errors; credentials via environment; limits.
  • Structured output validation: Schema; enum/constraints; invalid JSON; refusal; repair boundary.
  • Untrusted input boundaries: Document text versus instructions; evidence labels; tool output trust.
  • Small AI endpoint: One call; request IDs; output contract; no low-code dependency.

Common mistakes and debugging

Prompt only works for the demo

Use fixed ordinary, ambiguous and adversarial cases.

Schema equals truth

Validate output structure and separately evaluate grounding/correctness.

Blind retry loops

Retry only transient failures within a total deadline and record usage.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. What is prompt versioning?
  2. Why an adapter?
  3. Can JSON mode guarantee a correct answer?
Answer guide

What is prompt versioning?

Store template, examples, model/config and test results so comparisons are reproducible.

Why an adapter?

Provider SDK and transport details stay outside business logic and test contracts.

Can JSON mode guarantee a correct answer?

No; parseability, schema validity and factual correctness are different checks.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N04: Prompt / Context Engineering & Structured Outputs — official published module title.
  • N16: LLM Fundamentals & GenAI Landscape — official published module title.

L05 · Evaluation Engineering

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 2 · 14–22 focused hours · Difficulty 3/5 · P0

Before starting: L04 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B16 · C4 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

  • B16 · C4 — Read Now. Printed pp.160–178: task criteria and operational criteria. pp.200–207 were previewed in B03; review/apply, do not restart.

  • B03 · C4 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

3. Deterministic component checks
