Required reading:

  • B06 · F11 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B15 · C3 — Read Now. Printed pp.114–117 and 125–147: challenges, exact measures, similarity and AI judges. Defer entropy derivations.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: pytest fixtures. Read pytest’s fixtures introduction, fixture scope and teardown sections. Reuse L01 tests; design deterministic component assertions for your own failure cases.

Practice: Add exact checks that catch a valid-looking answer with an invalid citation.

Completion check: A fabricated citation ID fails even if the prose looks plausible.

Alternative · EN video + problem sets (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Human review and AI judges

Learn: Rubric anchors; blind labels; disagreement; bias; judge prompts.

Required reading:

  • B15 · C3 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B17 · F11 — Read Now. PDF pp.495–500: model-testing challenges; pp.523–528: behavioral tests. Reuse pytest knowledge from B06.

Primary · Book section: B15 · C3. Follow the assigned portion above; use the library references during implementation.

Practice: Label a small sample twice and compare judge/human disagreements by category.

Completion check: Human/judge disagreements are listed and analyzed, not hidden in an average.

Alternative · EN video + labs (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Retrieval versus answer metrics

Learn: Recall@k; MRR; faithfulness; answer relevance; no-evidence behavior.

Required reading:

  • B15 · C3 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B16 · C4 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: InformationRetrievalEvaluator. InformationRetrievalEvaluator section: Recall@k, MRR/NDCG and relevance-label inputs. Other evaluator classes are reference only.

Practice: Report retrieval and generation failures as separate tables.

Completion check: Retrieval and answer failures appear in separate result fields.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Regression gates and uncertainty

Learn: Counts; confidence caveats; stochastic repeats; release decision.

Required reading:

  • B03 · C4 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B17 · F11 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Build and test Python in GitHub Actions. Consult the Counts; confidence caveats; stochastic repeats; release decision sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Run a fixed eval suite and retain model/prompt/corpus versions with the results.

Completion check: A failed fixture blocks the gate and the result retains model/prompt/data versions.

Alternative · EN video + problem sets (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Include answerable, no-answer, multi-evidence, language, and security categories; review labels manually.
    • Pass: Do not call supplied examples unseen; preserve labels when chunk IDs change.
    • Pass: Calibrate a model judge against human review and document disagreements.
    • Pass: Record dataset/model/prompt/corpus versions, case count, latency, and cost or usage.
    • Pass: Implement retrieval metrics in L08 and tool/task metrics in L12; do not claim these experiments are already complete.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S3. Explain what success means and why. Public seed data is not holdout, mocks do not assess model quality, and judges are not the sole source of truth.

Keep: Authored evaluation data, rubric, and run manifest; A report showing real failures as well as successes.

What to defer

  • An elaborate evaluation platform before useful labels
  • Repeated tuning against the final holdout

python scripts/progress.py set L05 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

Technical Details · L05

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Working endpoint: Run the same request repeatedly and capture output/config.
  • Test separation: Distinguish HTTP correctness from answer usefulness.
  • Dataset hygiene: Keep development examples apart from release holdout.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Task criteria and failure taxonomy: Correctness; groundedness; abstention; privacy; severity.
  • Evaluation dataset design: Question strata; reference evidence; train/dev/holdout; versions.
  • Deterministic component checks: JSON schema; source IDs; ownership; parser contracts; golden fixtures.
  • Human review and AI judges: Rubric anchors; blind labels; disagreement; bias; judge prompts.
  • Retrieval versus answer metrics: Recall@k; MRR; faithfulness; answer relevance; no-evidence behavior.
  • Regression gates and uncertainty: Counts; confidence caveats; stochastic repeats; release decision.

Common mistakes and debugging

One combined RAG score

Measure retrieval, citations, generation and system behavior separately.

Judge treated as ground truth

Compare human/judge disagreement and inspect bias and failure modes.

Threshold invented after results

State intended thresholds before comparing candidates; report sample counts and uncertainty.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. What belongs in a holdout?
  2. Why calibrate an AI judge?
  3. What is an actionable eval failure?
Answer guide

What belongs in a holdout?

Cases not used to tune prompts, retrieval settings or model selection.

Why calibrate an AI judge?

A judge can favor style, verbosity or a model family and miss real task failures.

What is an actionable eval failure?

A labeled failure tied to a reproducible case and an identifiable component or product requirement.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N05: Software Testing & Full-Stack Debugging — official published module title.
  • N20: AI Evaluation, Safety & Responsible AI — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

The assessed exercise criteria are in the guided page. For evidence use EVIDENCE and EXPERIMENT.

L06 · PostgreSQL, ORM, and AI Application State

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 2 · 12–18 focused hours · Difficulty 2/5 · P1

You will learn: Move from Mongoose-style ODM experience to SQLAlchemy relations, sessions, transactions, and Alembic. Design document, conversation, and journal ownership and versions.

Before starting: L01 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B18 · F7 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Relational AI app model

Learn: User; document/version; chunk; journal; conversation/message; foreign keys.

Required reading:

  • B18 · F7 — Read Now. PDF pp.302–337: SQLAlchemy models, engine/session, CRUD, Alembic. Skim DB comparisons, known SQL and Prisma.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · From Mongo to Postgres + SQLAlchemy & Alembic. Only the linked “From Mongo to Postgres + SQLAlchemy & Alembic” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Draw an ERD with ownership and document version boundaries.

Completion check: An ERD has user ownership and document/chunk version relationships.

Alternative · EN video (optional) · Implementation reference

2. ORM queries and relationships

Learn: select; joins; eager loading; unit of work; pagination.

Required reading:

  • B18 · F7 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: SQLAlchemy Unified Tutorial. Consult the select; joins; eager loading; unit of work; pagination sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Implement a paginated owner-scoped document list and inspect generated SQL.

Completion check: The paginated query returns only the caller’s records.

Alternative · AR video (optional)

3. Async sessions and transactions

Learn: AsyncSession; commit/rollback; concurrent tasks; atomic writes.

Required reading:

  • B18 · F7 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: SQLAlchemy asyncio. Consult the AsyncSession; commit/rollback; concurrent tasks; atomic writes sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Force an exception mid-write and prove rollback; run concurrent independent requests.

Completion check: Failure mid-write rolls back; concurrent tasks do not share a session.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Schema evolution

Learn: Revision history; upgrades; downgrade/restore choice; data migration.

Required reading:

  • B18 · F7 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Alembic tutorial. Consult the Revision history; upgrades; downgrade/restore choice; data migration sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Upgrade a fresh database and a previous schema; verify expected rows remain.

Completion check: Fresh and previous-schema upgrades retain expected rows.

Alternative · AR video (optional)

5. Ownership and indexes

Learn: Composite keys; uniqueness; access paths; prevent cross-user joins.

Required reading:

  • B18 · F7 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Authorization cheat sheet. Consult the Composite keys; uniqueness; access paths; prevent cross-user joins sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Create two users with similar documents and prove no list/read/write leakage.

Completion check: Two-user direct and joined reads do not leak records.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

6. State consistency

Learn: Document hash/version; job state; cascade/delete policy; audit timestamps.

Required reading:

  • B18 · F7 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: SQLAlchemy Unified Tutorial. Read Working with Transactions and Data Manipulation with the ORM, especially flush/commit/rollback. Use those primitives to reason about failed job/state transitions; the tutorial does not supply an AI-ingestion state machine.

Practice: Retry a state change safely and test deletion behavior for related chunks/messages.

Completion check: Repeated ingestion/state updates do not create duplicate effects.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Produce an ERD and explain keys, nullability, uniqueness, and ownership boundaries.
    • Pass: A fresh database migrates successfully; transaction rollback leaves consistent state.
    • Pass: Concurrent tasks do not share one mutable session; cleanup is tested.
    • Pass: Explain generated joins and index choices without relearning basic SQL.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S3. Rebuild the schema from an empty database and prevent partial writes. Explain where transactions begin and end.

Keep: ERD, migrations, and real-database integration tests; Session/transaction lifecycle note.

What to defer

  • Multiple databases without a requirement
  • Repeating SQL or MongoDB fundamentals already demonstrated

Technical Details · L06

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • SQL transfer: Use joins, foreign keys and transactions without a new beginner course.
  • API prerequisite: Pass IDs and caller identity through a typed application layer.
  • Persistence reasoning: Explain how Mongo document ownership maps to relational keys.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Relational AI app model: User; document/version; chunk; journal; conversation/message; foreign keys.
  • ORM queries and relationships: select; joins; eager loading; unit of work; pagination.
  • Async sessions and transactions: AsyncSession; commit/rollback; concurrent tasks; atomic writes.
  • Schema evolution: Revision history; upgrades; downgrade/restore choice; data migration.
  • Ownership and indexes: Composite keys; uniqueness; access paths; prevent cross-user joins.
  • State consistency: Document hash/version; job state; cascade/delete policy; audit timestamps.

Common mistakes and debugging

AsyncSession shared across requests

Use one session per request/task and explicit transaction boundaries.

Unscoped get-by-ID

Every user-owned query includes caller ownership or an authorized join.

Migration only works on your laptop

Test upgrade from empty DB and from the previous schema snapshot.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. Session versus transaction?
  2. Why migrations instead of create_all in production?
  3. What makes ingestion idempotent?
Answer guide

Session versus transaction?

A session manages ORM state and work; a transaction defines the atomic database boundary.

Why migrations instead of create_all in production?

Changes must be versioned, reviewable and applied predictably to existing data.

What makes ingestion idempotent?

A stable identity/version key plus constraints and transaction logic prevent duplicate effects.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N06: SQL & NoSQL Database Engineering — official published module title.
  • N07: RESTful API, Backend Architecture & Security — official published module title.
  • N09: Software Architecture & System Design — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

The assessed exercise criteria are in the guided page. For evidence use EVIDENCE and EXPERIMENT.

L07 · RAG Document Preparation, Embeddings, and Semantic Search

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 3 · 16–24 focused hours · Difficulty 3/5 · P1

You will learn: Convert a small textual PDF into traceable chunks with page provenance. Compare a lexical baseline with embeddings appropriate to the application language.

Before starting: L03, L06 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B21 · F5 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Parsing and extraction quality

Learn: Text PDF; page order; tables; scanned PDF limitations; extraction checks.

Required reading:

  • B21 · F5 — Read Now. PDF pp.219–236: document preparation, upload, extraction, chunking, embeddings and vector storage. Web scraping pp.213–218 is optional.
  • B22 · C8 — Skim. Printed pp.396–402: processing principles applied to the document corpus. B07 quality is review only.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: PyMuPDF RAG extraction. Consult the Text PDF; page order; tables; scanned PDF limitations; extraction checks sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Extract three pages and manually compare text/order/page numbers with the originals.

Completion check: Three extracted pages retain correct ordering and page identifiers.

Alternative · AR video (optional)

2. Chunk design and metadata

Learn: Size; overlap; headings; stable IDs; source/page/version.

Required reading:

  • B19 · G4 — Read Now. Printed pp.74–82: retrieval, embeddings and grounding intuition. The improvement/code slice is B24.
  • B21 · F5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · File Processing. Only the linked “File Processing” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Compare two chunk policies and retain exact source offsets or page references.

Completion check: Both chunk policies preserve source/version metadata.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Embeddings and multilingual behavior

Learn: Bi-encoders; query/document encoding; model card; prefixes; dimensions.

Required reading:

  • B20 · C6 — Read Now. Printed pp.253–266: retrieval foundations. No agent/memory reading yet.
  • B23 · C3 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Sentence Transformers semantic search. Consult the Bi-encoders; query/document encoding; model card; prefixes; dimensions sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Embed Arabic and English queries with the documented prefixes and inspect top results.

Completion check: Model-card query/passage prefixes are applied to the bilingual test set.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Vector persistence and exact search

Learn: pgvector types; distance operators; deterministic test corpus.

Required reading:

  • B21 · F5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · The way to PgVector. Only the linked “The way to PgVector” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Run exact top-k queries in SQL and verify against a small in-memory baseline.

Completion check: SQL exact top-k agrees with the small deterministic baseline.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Filtering and lexical baseline

Learn: Owner/document predicates; full-text search; language configuration.

Required reading:

  • B20 · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: PostgreSQL text search controls. Consult the Owner/document predicates; full-text search; language configuration sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Compare keyword and vector retrieval; test tenant isolation before returning chunks.

Completion check: Owner filters apply before results are returned; lexical/vector results are compared.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Repeatable ingestion lifecycle

Learn: Content hash; versions; retries; delete/re-index; stale vectors.

Required reading:

  • B21 · F5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B22 · C8 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: SQLAlchemy Unified Tutorial. Review SQLAlchemy transaction/session examples only. Design source hashes, ingestion versions and retry state in your application; these AI lifecycle decisions are your exercise.

Practice: Ingest the same document twice, change its version, delete it, and assert no stale duplicates.

Completion check: Retry, version change and deletion leave no stale duplicate vectors.

Alternative · AR video (optional)

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Preserve PDF page, printed label if available, and offsets before cleaning.
    • Pass: Unsupported inputs have a clear failure reason rather than an empty ready index.
    • Pass: Save chunker, document, model, and dimension; never mix incompatible embedding spaces.
    • Pass: Evaluate representative queries, including cross-language cases if they belong to the product scope.
    • Pass: No duplicate chunks, no partially published version, and a clear deletion contract for L11.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S3. Every chunk maps to its source. Reprocessing is safe, embedding dimensions match storage, and the implementation remains small enough to debug.

Keep: Ingestion functions or CLI, corpus manifest, and provenance tests; Lexical and dense retrieval baseline.

What to defer

  • Distributed processing without scale
  • Premature ANN or OCR

Record progress and choose the next Level

python scripts/progress.py set L07 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

Unlocks: L08

Technical Details · L07

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Database readiness: Persist owner/document/version/chunk metadata with tested ownership.
  • Vector readiness: Explain dot product and cosine and distinguish similarity from probability.
  • Corpus readiness: Use a small permitted textual corpus and list parsing limitations.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Parsing and extraction quality: Text PDF; page order; tables; scanned PDF limitations; extraction checks.
  • Chunk design and metadata: Size; overlap; headings; stable IDs; source/page/version.
  • Embeddings and multilingual behavior: Bi-encoders; query/document encoding; model card; prefixes; dimensions.
  • Vector persistence and exact search: pgvector types; distance operators; deterministic test corpus.
  • Filtering and lexical baseline: Owner/document predicates; full-text search; language configuration.
  • Repeatable ingestion lifecycle: Content hash; versions; retries; delete/re-index; stale vectors.

Common mistakes and debugging

Text without provenance

Keep source ID, page, chunk ordinal, version and owner throughout ingestion.

Choosing an English-only embedding blindly

Test Arabic/English queries and follow model-card preprocessing.

ANN before a baseline

Use exact search to establish correctness and measure before adding an index.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. Why overlap chunks?
  2. Why store an embedding model version?
  3. Can you filter after returning candidates?
Answer guide

Why overlap chunks?

It can preserve boundary context but increases duplicates, cost and retrieval redundancy.

Why store an embedding model version?

Changing models or dimensions invalidates comparisons and may require re-indexing.

Can you filter after returning candidates?

Ownership must be enforced in retrieval; post-filtering alone can leak data or destroy recall.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N12: Data Preparation & Feature Engineering — official published module title.
  • N17: Embeddings, Vector DBs & Semantic Search — official published module title.
  • N19: RAG Systems Engineering — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

L08 · RAG Systems, Citations, and Retrieval Improvement

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 3 · 18–28 focused hours · Difficulty 3/5 · P1

You will learn: Build retrieval → context → generation with verifiable citations. Diagnose failures and justify hybrid search or reranking through comparison.

Before starting: L05, L07 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B24 · G4 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. RAG orchestration

Learn: Question → retrieve → assemble context → generate → validate → cite.

Required reading:

  • B24 · G4 — Read Now. Printed pp.94–100: improvement ideas and code explanation. Do not restart pp.74–82.
  • B25 · F5 — Read Now. PDF pp.237–239: retrieval into generation and the integrated RAG answer flow. Reuse B21 ingestion code.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · Augmented Answers. Only the linked “Augmented Answers” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Ship /ask with passages, source IDs and a defined no-evidence response.

Completion check: /ask returns permitted passages and a defined no-evidence response.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

2. Citation correctness

Learn: Allowed IDs; source resolution; page display; claim support.

Required reading:

  • B26 · F11 — Read Now. PDF pp.519–522: integration tests, particularly retrieval/vector-database boundaries. Behavioral testing pp.523–528 is B17 review.

Primary · Documentation: assertions reference: Assertions and metrics. The assertion type matching Citation correctness; start with deterministic checks, then calibrated model grading if needed. Do not adopt all plugins.

Practice: Inject fabricated source IDs and unrelated real sources; catch both failure types.

Completion check: Both a fake ID and an unrelated real citation are caught.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Ranking evaluation

Learn: Recall@k; MRR/NDCG; relevant-document versus relevant-chunk labels.

  • B20 · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B27 · C6 — Read Now. Printed pp.267–274: retrieval improvements and tradeoffs. pp.253–266 are B20 review.

Completion check: Manual MRR/Recall example agrees with the evaluator.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

4. Hybrid search

Learn: Lexical + dense; candidate union; reciprocal rank fusion; filters.

Required reading:

  • B27 · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official repository / implementation guide: pgvector README. Consult the Lexical + dense; candidate union; reciprocal rank fusion; filters sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Compare lexical-only, vector-only and hybrid on identical owner-filtered queries.

Completion check: Lexical, dense and hybrid use the same labeled queries and owner filters.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Reranking and query changes

Learn: Cross-encoder; candidate count; query rewrite; multilingual checks.

Required reading:

  • B27 · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Retrieve and rerank. Consult the Cross-encoder; candidate count; query rewrite; multilingual checks sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Add reranking only if evidence ranking improves enough to justify extra latency.

Completion check: The quality gain and added reranking latency are reported together.

Alternative · EN video + labs (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Answer reliability and ablations

Learn: Abstention; conflicting sources; context-only checks; retriever/generator failures.

Required reading:

  • B15 · C3 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B16 · C4 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B17 · F11 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Documentation / evaluation tutorials: Ragas evaluation documentation. Consult the Abstention; conflicting sources; context-only checks; retriever/generator failures sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Freeze a baseline, change one factor, report quality/citation/latency plus three failed cases.

Completion check: One-factor ablation includes failed cases, citation checks and latency.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Reject invented IDs and review whether cited passages actually support claims.
    • Pass: Distinguish parsing, retrieval, and generation failures.
    • Pass: Keep the same dataset and report quality, latency, and cost differences.
    • Pass: Save fixed settings and negative results rather than presenting only wins.
    • Pass: The system does not invent support; similarity is not interpreted as answer confidence.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S3. Explain five failures using evidence. References both exist and support the answer under review. Report thresholds as your project targets, not official ITI standards.

Keep: Measured RAG MVP and citation contract tests; Before/after report and failure taxonomy.

What to defer

  • Agentic retrieval before a measured baseline
  • Fine-tuning as a parser repair strategy

Technical Details · L08

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • L05 readiness: A labeled query set and separate component metrics exist.
  • L07 readiness: Each returned chunk has valid owner/source/page metadata.
  • Prompt readiness: A model adapter returns validated structured fields.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • RAG orchestration: Question → retrieve → assemble context → generate → validate → cite.
  • Citation correctness: Allowed IDs; source resolution; page display; claim support.
  • Ranking evaluation: Recall@k; MRR/NDCG; relevant-document versus relevant-chunk labels.
  • Hybrid search: Lexical + dense; candidate union; reciprocal rank fusion; filters.
  • Reranking and query changes: Cross-encoder; candidate count; query rewrite; multilingual checks.
  • Answer reliability and ablations: Abstention; conflicting sources; context-only checks; retriever/generator failures.

Common mistakes and debugging

A citation string equals evidence

Resolve source IDs, inspect source content and assess support for the actual claim.

Hybrid search always improves

Use the same query set and compare recall/ranking/cost; retain a simpler baseline if better.

Reranking the entire corpus

Retrieve candidates first; rerank a bounded list and measure the latency increase.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. What does Recall@k tell you?
  2. Why RRF?
  3. What must abstention test?
Answer guide

What does Recall@k tell you?

How much of the labeled relevant evidence appears among the first k results; define relevance granularity.

Why RRF?

It combines ranked lists without assuming their numeric scores are calibrated to each other.

What must abstention test?

No evidence, irrelevant evidence and conflicting evidence; the response should expose uncertainty rather than invent support.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N17: Embeddings, Vector DBs & Semantic Search — official published module title.
  • N19: RAG Systems Engineering — official published module title.
  • N20: AI Evaluation, Safety & Responsible AI — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

The assessed exercise criteria are in the guided page. For evidence use EVIDENCE and EXPERIMENT.

L09 · Context Engineering, Memory, and Conversations

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 3 · 14–22 focused hours · Difficulty 3/5 · P1

You will learn: Budget instructions, evidence, selected journals, history, and memory. Manage memory provenance, correction, deletion, and summarization loss.

Before starting: L08 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Context engineering and context rot

Learn: Prompt versus context; attention budget; irrelevant tokens; position sensitivity.

Required reading:

  • B30 · C5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Engineering article: Effective context engineering for AI agents. Read the opening distinction between prompt and context engineering, then “Why context engineering is important” on context rot. Save the later techniques for their steps below.

Practice: Compare focused evidence with the same evidence buried in distractors and report task outcomes.

Completion check: Compare task outcomes with focused versus distractor-heavy context.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

2. Context budget and selection

Learn: Instructions; schema; evidence; journal; history; output reserve.

Required reading:

  • B29 · C10 — Read Now. Printed p.450: Enhance Context. Other C10 sections have later owners.
  • B30 · C5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Engineering article: Effective context engineering for AI agents. Read “The anatomy of effective context” (system prompts, tools, examples and history). Apply its selection principle to your own explicit token-budget manifest; the article does not prescribe numeric budgets.

Practice: Create a context manifest with token counts, selection reasons and source IDs.

Completion check: A context manifest shows token use, source IDs and selection reasons.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Just-in-time retrieval

Learn: References versus full content; selective journal search; minimal tool outputs.

Required reading:

  • B29 · C10 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Engineering article: Effective context engineering for AI agents. Read “Context retrieval and agentic search”, especially just-in-time loading and progressive discovery. Compare the two retrieval strategies in your own application.

Practice: Fetch relevant journal records on demand and prove unrelated entries are excluded.

Completion check: An unrelated journal is excluded from the model context.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

4. Conversation history and structured memory

Learn: Working history; durable facts; provenance; conflicts; user edit/delete.

Required reading:

  • B28 · C6 — Read Now. Printed pp.300–304: Memory. Keep agent pp.275–299 for B36.

Primary · Machine-translated lesson / article: Agent memory · Arabic. Only the linked translated lesson, particularly its sections about Conversation history and structured memory. Its English video/cloud build is an alternative, not an extra requirement.

Practice: Store a user-approved memory with source, timestamp and correction/deletion tests.

Completion check: Memory can be corrected/deleted and retains its source/version.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Compaction and retained constraints

Learn: Summaries; checkpoints; recoverable sources; lost-detail evaluation.

Required reading:

  • B28 · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Engineering article: Effective context engineering for AI agents. Read only “Compaction” and “Structured note-taking” under context engineering for long-horizon tasks. Test what your summary loses; do not implement sub-agents here.

Practice: Summarize a long synthetic conversation and test five facts/constraints before and after.

Completion check: Five constraints/facts are checked before and after compaction.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

6. Sub-agent boundary

Learn: Partitionable tasks; narrow context; summary handoff; coordination cost.

Required reading:

  • B28 · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Engineering article: Building effective agents. Read the orchestrator-workers pattern and the closing guidance on keeping designs simple. Compare with Anthropic’s context article’s sub-agent discussion; this is a design exercise, not a multi-agent build.

Practice: Write an ADR choosing a single workflow unless a bounded comparison demonstrates value.

Completion check: The ADR names a measured trigger for adding a sub-agent.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Show how each input is selected, bounded, and truncated.
    • Pass: Use the same tasks and measure effects rather than assuming more context helps.
    • Pass: Clearly separate source claims, journal facts, and assistant suggestions.
    • Pass: Do not infer permanent personality traits automatically; changes affect active context.
    • Pass: Compare required facts before/after and retain source links and covered message ranges; JIT tools arrive in L12.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S4. Explain what entered context and why. Deletion is respected and stale summaries are not silently treated as facts.

Keep: Context contract, journal workflow, and editable memory schema; Focused-context and optional compaction experiments.

What to defer

  • Uneditable automatic memory
  • Sub-agents before a working bounded single-agent design

Technical Details · L09

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • RAG readiness: Explain which passages were retrieved and which reached the prompt.
  • State readiness: Conversations and journals are owner-scoped and deletable.
  • Evaluation readiness: Have multi-turn cases with expected facts and source evidence.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Context engineering and context rot: Prompt versus context; attention budget; irrelevant tokens; position sensitivity.
  • Context budget and selection: Instructions; schema; evidence; journal; history; output reserve.
  • Just-in-time retrieval: References versus full content; selective journal search; minimal tool outputs.
  • Conversation history and structured memory: Working history; durable facts; provenance; conflicts; user edit/delete.
  • Compaction and retained constraints: Summaries; checkpoints; recoverable sources; lost-detail evaluation.
  • Sub-agent boundary: Partitionable tasks; narrow context; summary handoff; coordination cost.

Common mistakes and debugging

Append all history forever

Allocate a budget and retrieve only relevant state.

Summary treated as canonical truth

Link memories to sources and preserve correction/deletion behavior.

Multi-agent complexity for memory alone

Prove a single workflow fails before adding another agent.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. Prompt versus context engineering?
  2. What is just-in-time retrieval?
  3. When is compaction acceptable?
Answer guide

Prompt versus context engineering?

Prompt writing is part of choosing and managing all instructions, tools, evidence, history and state available to the model.

What is just-in-time retrieval?

Keep lightweight references and fetch detailed information only when the current step needs it.

When is compaction acceptable?

After measuring retained facts, lost constraints and future-task performance with recoverable source state.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N04: Prompt / Context Engineering & Structured Outputs — official published module title.
  • N19: RAG Systems Engineering — official published module title.
  • N22: Agentic AI & Multi-Agent Orchestration — official published module title.

L10 · Concurrency, Streaming, and Long-Running Jobs

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 4 · 16–24 focused hours · Difficulty 3/5 · P1

You will learn: Separate I/O concurrency from CPU parallelism and avoid blocking the event loop. Handle streaming cancellation, timeouts, and restartable ingestion jobs.

Before starting: L08 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B31 · F5 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Event loop execution

Learn: await; sync call boundaries; blocking demo; I/O versus CPU.

Required reading:

  • B31 · F5 — Read Now. PDF pp.187–210: concurrency/async foundations; pp.253–256: background work/summary. pp.211–212 were B13; pp.240–252 advanced inference is optional in L17.

Primary · Single video: Next-Level Concurrent Programming in Python with Asyncio. The single linked video. No other channel/playlist content is assigned.

Practice: Run concurrent requests to a sleeping/blocking fake provider and compare latency.

Completion check: Concurrent request measurements expose and then remove the blocking call.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

2. Timeouts and bounded parallelism

Learn: Total deadlines; per-stage timeouts; semaphore/pool; retry backoff.

Required reading:

  • B31 · F5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: asyncio tasks. Consult the Total deadlines; per-stage timeouts; semaphore/pool; retry backoff sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Bound in-flight provider calls and test timeout plus cancellation without leaked tasks.

Completion check: Timeout/cancel tests leave no leaked tasks or unbounded requests.

Alternative · EN video (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Streaming protocol

Learn: StreamingResponse; SSE framing; partial output; completion/error events.

Required reading:

  • B32 · F6 — Read Now. PDF pp.258–269: protocol choice/polling/SSE; pp.278–290: stream/SSE server and client. Skim comparison pp.270–277; WebSocket build pp.291–301 is optional.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: FastAPI custom responses. Consult the StreamingResponse; SSE framing; partial output; completion/error events sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Build a stream consumer test that receives incremental events and one terminal event.

Completion check: Incremental events have valid framing and one terminal event.

Implementation reference

4. Cancellation and disconnects

Learn: Client abort; generator cleanup; provider stop; partial persistence.

Required reading:

  • B32 · F6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: asyncio tasks. Consult the Client abort; generator cleanup; provider stop; partial persistence sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Disconnect mid-response; verify cleanup and a defined partial-message state.

Completion check: A disconnect closes resources and defines the partial-message state.

Alternative · EN (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Background versus durable jobs

Learn: In-process work; broker/worker; queued/running/failed; restart recovery.

Required reading:

  • B31 · F5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · Celery Workers 1/2. Only the linked “Celery Workers 1/2” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Move document processing into a worker and observe job status via the API.

Completion check: A restarted worker exposes consistent persisted job state.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Idempotent retries and worker tests

Learn: Acks; duplicate delivery; poisoned inputs; retry budget; dead-letter policy.

Required reading:

  • B31 · F5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Celery task semantics. Consult the Acks; duplicate delivery; poisoned inputs; retry budget; dead-letter policy sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Kill/restart a worker, retry the same job and verify no duplicate chunks or false success.

Completion check: Duplicate delivery creates no duplicate chunks or false success.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Demonstrate a blocking mistake and a suitable correction; async alone does not solve CPU-heavy parsing.
    • Pass: Test disconnect, partial provider failure, time limits, and cleanup.
    • Pass: Replay duplicate messages and restart a worker without duplicating chunks.
    • Pass: Test actual crash behavior; BackgroundTasks is not a durable queue and exactly-once is not assumed.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S4. Light endpoints remain responsive during I/O waits. Partial output is not presented as complete success, and failed indexing never replaces a ready version.

Keep: Concurrency experiment, streaming tests, and job lifecycle; Replay and crash-recovery evidence.

What to defer

  • Additional messaging/workflow platforms without a requirement
  • WebSockets solely because token streaming is needed

Technical Details · L10

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • API readiness: Fake slow/error providers exist so tests are reproducible.
  • Persistence readiness: A job has stable IDs and persisted states.
  • Concurrency basics: Explain I/O waiting versus CPU work.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Event loop execution: await; sync call boundaries; blocking demo; I/O versus CPU.
  • Timeouts and bounded parallelism: Total deadlines; per-stage timeouts; semaphore/pool; retry backoff.
  • Streaming protocol: StreamingResponse; SSE framing; partial output; completion/error events.
  • Cancellation and disconnects: Client abort; generator cleanup; provider stop; partial persistence.
  • Background versus durable jobs: In-process work; broker/worker; queued/running/failed; restart recovery.
  • Idempotent retries and worker tests: Acks; duplicate delivery; poisoned inputs; retry budget; dead-letter policy.

Common mistakes and debugging

async around blocking code

A sync parser/model call still blocks; isolate it explicitly.

Retry duplicates writes

Use stable job identity and transactional effects.

Browser stops but provider continues

Propagate cancellation and record abandoned work and usage.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. When is BackgroundTasks insufficient?
  2. What does at-least-once imply?
  3. Why bound concurrency?
Answer guide

When is BackgroundTasks insufficient?

When work must survive a process restart, needs retries/queues or long-running execution.

What does at-least-once imply?

The same task may execute more than once; effects must be idempotent.

Why bound concurrency?

To prevent pool exhaustion, provider rate-limit storms and unbounded memory usage.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N02: Advanced Python Programming for AI Engineers — official published module title.
  • N18: Asynchronous Processing & Streaming — official published module title.
  • N25: LLMOps & Production Readiness for AI Applications — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

The assessed exercise criteria are in the guided page. For evidence use EVIDENCE and EXPERIMENT.

Technical Details · L11

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Auth transfer: Explain JWT validation and role versus object permission.
  • RAG readiness: Document retrieval and source display are separate access paths.
  • Job readiness: Job IDs and state can be tied to a caller.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • FastAPI authentication: Hashing; token verification; expiry; dependencies; cookie/header choice.
  • Object-level authorization: Owner predicates; chunks; sources; messages; memories; jobs.
  • Prompt injection and excessive agency: Malicious documents; tool output instructions; least privilege.
  • Upload and parser security: Size/type limits; path traversal; storage names; resource limits.
  • Privacy, deletion and observability: Journal sensitivity; consent; redaction; retention; cache/vector cleanup.
  • Responsible answer behavior: Attribution; uncertainty; book claim versus suggestion; feedback/appeal.

Common mistakes and debugging

Prompt as an access-control system

Permission checks execute in backend code before data/tools are exposed.

Sanitize then trust everything

Treat retrieved text/tool output as untrusted throughout execution.

Log all prompts for debugging

Redact sensitive fields and define retention/erasure behavior.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. Authentication versus authorization?
  2. Why indirect prompt injection?
  3. What is a safe reflection boundary?
Answer guide

Authentication versus authorization?

Identity verification versus permission to perform a specific action on a specific object.

Why indirect prompt injection?

A document or tool result can carry instructions even when the user query is benign.

What is a safe reflection boundary?

Separate cited book content, personal journal facts and generated suggestions; avoid unsupported clinical claims.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N07: RESTful API, Backend Architecture & Security — official published module title.
  • N20: AI Evaluation, Safety & Responsible AI — official published module title.
  • N26: AI Security, Governance & Compliance — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

The assessed exercise criteria are in the guided page. For evidence use EVIDENCE and EXPERIMENT.

L12 · Tool Calling, Bounded Agents, and Multi-Agent Decisions

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 4 · 18–28 focused hours · Difficulty 4/5 · P1

You will learn: Distinguish fixed workflows from model-directed tool loops. Implement safe tool contracts and evaluate tool/task failures. Understand sub-agent patterns without assuming they improve the product.

Before starting: L05, L09, L11 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B36 · G5 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Agent anatomy and design choice

Learn: State; model decision; action; observation; workflow alternatives.

Required reading:

  • B36 · G5 — Read Now. Printed pp.102–107 and 122–127: tools, agents and code explanation. Skip repeated travel-app setup.
  • B36a · G6 — Read Now. Printed pp.130–132: patterns first. Remaining implementation pp.133–171 is Optional Advanced after a measured need.

Primary · Engineering article: Building effective agents. Read the “workflows versus agents” distinction and the pattern/tool-design portion matching Agent anatomy and design choice. Multi-agent implementations are not required.

Practice: Draw a fixed workflow and tool loop for the same journal question; choose a baseline.

Completion check: A workflow baseline and bounded-agent alternative have explicit control-flow diagrams.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

2. Tool design and schemas

Learn: Small names; typed inputs; concise outputs; errors; idempotent reads.

Required reading:

  • B36b · C6 — Read Now. Printed pp.275–299: tools, planning and agent failure modes. Retrieval/memory slices were B20/B27/B28.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Ollama tool calling. Consult the Small names; typed inputs; concise outputs; errors; idempotent reads sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Expose one owner-scoped search tool; reject malformed and unauthorized arguments.

Completion check: Tool arguments and caller permissions are checked before execution.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Bounded execution loop

Learn: Max steps; timeout; token/cost budget; stop/ask-human; error states.

Required reading:

  • B36b · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Book section: B36b · C6. Follow the assigned portion above; use the library references during implementation.

Practice: Test repeated calls, unavailable tools, budget exhaustion and terminal behavior.

Completion check: Repeated calls and budget exhaustion terminate predictably.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Agent evaluation

Learn: Tool choice; arguments; trajectory; task success; final-answer grounding.

Required reading:

  • B17 · F11 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B36b · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Documentation: assertions reference: Assertions and metrics. The assertion type matching Agent evaluation; start with deterministic checks, then calibrated model grading if needed. Do not adopt all plugins.

Practice: Label five correct and five failed traces; report tool and task metrics separately.

Completion check: Tool/trajectory scores are distinct from final-answer quality.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

5. Memory and context interfaces

Learn: Retrieve only needed state; observation size; provenance; side-effect confirmation.

Required reading:

  • B28 · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Engineering article: Effective context engineering for AI agents. Review only tool design, just-in-time retrieval and sub-agent tradeoffs from L09; no full article reread.

Practice: Limit tool result size and prove relevant evidence survives the bound.

Completion check: Bounded tool results preserve the relevant evidence with source IDs.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

6. Multi-agent comparison

Learn: Specialist role; independent context; handoff summary; coordination failures.

Required reading:

  • B36a · G6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B36b · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Engineering article: Building effective agents. Read the “workflows versus agents” distinction and the pattern/tool-design portion matching Multi-agent comparison. Multi-agent implementations are not required.

Practice: Run one partitioned experiment against the single-agent baseline; record a go/no-go ADR.

Completion check: The comparison records any benefit plus coordination cost/failures.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Validate schemas and enforce authenticated ownership; no generic SQL or shell tool.
    • Pass: Handle unknown tool, invalid arguments, no-tool-needed, and repeated-loop cases.
    • Pass: Report success, tool errors, unauthorized attempts, latency, and cost.
    • Pass: Only permitted requested content is loaded; compare against upfront retrieval.
    • Pass: Implement extra agents only if a measured use case justifies isolation, coordination, and cost.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S5. A model request is never treated as authorization. Loops stop predictably and you can justify either using an agent or retaining a fixed workflow.

Keep: Tool contracts, bounded agent loop, and evaluation report; Single-agent versus multi-agent ADR.

What to defer

  • Framework orchestration before understanding a loop
  • Multi-agent implementation just to check a syllabus keyword

Record progress and choose the next Level

python scripts/progress.py set L12 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

Unlocks: L13, L16

Resource Library · L12 — Tool Calling, Bounded Agents, and Multi-Agent Decisions

← Follow the learning path · Technical details · Master book order

This is the complete retained library, not a second to-do list. Follow Primary resources in the README; everything else is an Alternative, Reference or Optional Advanced resource. You do not need to watch every video or finish every course.

Selection key

  • Primary: used by at least one numbered step; follow only its assigned portion.
  • Alternative: optional Arabic/English explanation or practice replacing a primary explanation.
  • Reference: consult a named API/spec section when implementing.
  • Optional Advanced: a later deep dive; does not block this Level.

Resource Type describes the format, independently of its role. A single video containing a playlist parameter remains a single video. Exact titles/numbers are used where verified; unverified identifiers are explicitly marked.

Arabic availability: Arabic translated agent lessons provide structured text. A complete Arabic course covering bounded loops plus trajectory evaluation was not verified; pair them with the English agent course and assessment labs.

Book references

Assigned reading is authoritative in the master order for L12. The chapter map preserves full chapter/page locations and split reasons. This library does not assign those chapters again.

This Level’s ledger IDs: B36 · G5, B36a · G6, B36b · C6.

Previously covered / lookup only: B17 · F11, B28 · C6. No mandatory chapter restart.

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • RAG/security readiness: A caller-scoped search operation exists and rejects unauthorized access.
  • Contract readiness: Validate tool arguments and distinguish error from empty success.
  • Evaluation readiness: Measure task completion and tool behavior separately.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Agent anatomy and design choice: State; model decision; action; observation; workflow alternatives.
  • Tool design and schemas: Small names; typed inputs; concise outputs; errors; idempotent reads.
  • Bounded execution loop: Max steps; timeout; token/cost budget; stop/ask-human; error states.
  • Agent evaluation: Tool choice; arguments; trajectory; task success; final-answer grounding.
  • Memory and context interfaces: Retrieve only needed state; observation size; provenance; side-effect confirmation.
  • Multi-agent comparison: Specialist role; independent context; handoff summary; coordination failures.

Common mistakes and debugging

Every problem needs an agent

Compare with deterministic routing/workflows first.

Tool execution trusts model arguments

Validate schema and permission for every call, independent of model compliance.

Unbounded self-correction

Enforce steps, wall time and token/cost budgets in code.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. Workflow versus agent?
  2. What is a trajectory failure?
  3. When add sub-agents?
Answer guide

Workflow versus agent?

The workflow fixes control flow in code; the agent chooses next actions within enforced limits.

What is a trajectory failure?

An invalid/unnecessary/repeated tool sequence even if the final prose looks plausible.

When add sub-agents?

A separable task and a measured improvement justify coordination, isolation and extra cost.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N20: AI Evaluation, Safety & Responsible AI — official published module title.
  • N22: Agentic AI & Multi-Agent Orchestration — official published module title.
  • N24: AI Workflow Automation & Business Process Integration — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

The assessed exercise criteria are in the guided page. For evidence use EVIDENCE and EXPERIMENT.

L13 · MCP, Enterprise Integration, and AI Workflows

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 4 · 10–16 focused hours · Difficulty 3/5 · P2

You will learn: Understand MCP host/client/server roles, primitives, transport, and authorization. Build one bounded integration with explicit failure behavior.

Before starting: L12 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B37 · C10 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. MCP architecture and lifecycle

Learn: Host; client; server; initialization; capability discovery.

Required reading:

  • B37 · C10 — Read Now. Printed pp.472–473: orchestration. G8 pp.211–240 is optional conceptual comparison; MCP itself uses current official docs.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: MCP architecture. Consult the Host; client; server; initialization; capability discovery sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Diagram one connection and identify the boundary that owns user authorization.

Completion check: The host/client/server diagram identifies the authorization owner.

Alternative · AR translated curriculum (optional)

2. Tools and resources

Learn: Input/output schemas; resource URIs; errors; prompt templates.

Required reading:

  • B36b · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Build an MCP server. Consult the Input/output schemas; resource URIs; errors; prompt templates sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Implement one read-only document search capability with explicit response limits.

Completion check: One read-only tool has typed inputs, bounded output and deterministic fixtures.

Alternative · AR translated curriculum (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Client and transport

Learn: stdio first; remote transport when justified; discovery/call; cleanup.

Required reading:

  • B37 · C10 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Build an MCP client. Consult the stdio first; remote transport when justified; discovery/call; cleanup sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Run a client-server integration test with pinned versions and deterministic fixtures.

Completion check: A pinned-version client discovers and calls the local server.

Alternative · AR translated curriculum (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Authorization and trust

Learn: Caller context; least privilege; token audience; no token passthrough.

Required reading:

  • B35 · C5 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: MCP security best practices. Consult the Caller context; least privilege; token audience; no token passthrough sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Test cross-user queries and a tool result containing instruction-like text.

Completion check: Cross-user calls and instruction-like server output cannot bypass policy.

Alternative · AR translated curriculum (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Enterprise adapter contracts

Learn: Timeout; retries; schema drift; pagination; safe error handling.

Required reading:

  • B36b · C6 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: HTTPX timeouts. Read HTTPX’s connect/read/write/pool timeouts and Timeout configuration example. Apply them to a narrow enterprise adapter; authorization and adapter schemas remain your design work from L11/L12.

Practice: Fake an upstream outage and incompatible schema; preserve explicit failure status.

Completion check: Outage/schema-drift fixtures return explicit failures, not empty success.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Orchestration and observability

Learn: Business workflow versus protocol; tracing; audit; disconnect/reconnect.

Required reading:

  • B37 · C10 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Python instrumentation. Consult the Business workflow versus protocol; tracing; audit; disconnect/reconnect sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Trace one MCP call end-to-end and test shutdown/reconnection without duplicate effects.

Completion check: Shutdown/reconnect tests avoid duplicate effects and retain trace correlation.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Identify who manages context and authorization; neither is delegated to the protocol.
    • Pass: Pin the protocol/SDK version and record successful discovery and invocation.
    • Pass: Connection failure does not widen permissions.
    • Pass: The application enforces approval, authorization, and duplicate handling for writes; no external message needs to be sent.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S5. Demonstrate one real tool invocation through a client and explain where permissions, context, approval, and retries live.

Keep: Small MCP server/client demo and integration diagram; Version record and permission/timeout tests.

What to defer

  • Many MCP servers or invented enterprise integrations
  • Production remote authentication before a local protocol demonstration

Selection key

  • Primary: used by at least one numbered step; follow only its assigned portion.
  • Alternative: optional Arabic/English explanation or practice replacing a primary explanation.
  • Reference: consult a named API/spec section when implementing.
  • Optional Advanced: a later deep dive; does not block this Level.

Resource Type describes the format, independently of its role. A single video containing a playlist parameter remains a single video. Exact titles/numbers are used where verified; unverified identifiers are explicitly marked.

Arabic availability: The verified Arabic MCP resource is a translated curriculum with code links. No comparable Egyptian video course was verified; use the Anthropic course plus official SDK examples for implementation.

Technical Details · L13

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Tool prerequisites: A typed read-only operation already works without MCP.
  • Security prerequisites: Caller identity and tool/data permissions are explicit.
  • Protocol reasoning: Distinguish app orchestration, transport and model behavior.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • MCP architecture and lifecycle: Host; client; server; initialization; capability discovery.
  • Tools and resources: Input/output schemas; resource URIs; errors; prompt templates.
  • Client and transport: stdio first; remote transport when justified; discovery/call; cleanup.
  • Authorization and trust: Caller context; least privilege; token audience; no token passthrough.
  • Enterprise adapter contracts: Timeout; retries; schema drift; pagination; safe error handling.
  • Orchestration and observability: Business workflow versus protocol; tracing; audit; disconnect/reconnect.

Common mistakes and debugging

MCP grants permission automatically

Protocol interoperability is separate from authorization.

SDK example without version pin

Pin SDK/protocol and document transport compatibility.

Remote server output becomes trusted instructions

Validate and scope results; keep business decisions in application code.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. Host/client/server?
  2. Tools versus resources?
  3. Why not add MCP everywhere?
Answer guide

Host/client/server?

The host application manages model interaction, the client maintains a server connection, and the server exposes capabilities.

Tools versus resources?

Tools perform callable operations; resources expose addressable context. Neither bypasses permissions.

Why not add MCP everywhere?

Use it for interoperable external integrations; direct internal calls can be simpler.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N23: Model Context Protocol & Enterprise Tool Integration — official published module title.
  • N24: AI Workflow Automation & Business Process Integration — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.
