L14 · MLOps, LLMOps, Architecture, and Deployment

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 5 · 20–30 focused hours · Difficulty 4/5 · P2

You will learn: Version model/prompt/corpus/evaluation artifacts and trace execution. Release, restore, and diagnose an AI service and its worker.

Before starting: L10, L11 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B39 · F12 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Containers and service topology

Learn: API/worker/db; images; networks; volumes; configuration; health.

Required reading:

  • B39 · F12 — Read Now. PDF pp.536–594: images, persistence, networks, Compose and deployment. Skim familiar Docker basics.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Docker workshop. Consult the API/worker/db; images; networks; volumes; configuration; health sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Start the stack from a clean checkout and preserve uploaded documents/database state across restarts.

Completion check: The complete stack starts cleanly and retains persisted state after restart.

Alternative · AR video (optional)

2. CI and evaluation gates

Learn: Install pinned deps; lint/types if adopted; tests; fixed eval; artifacts.

Required reading:

  • B38 · F11 — Read Now. PDF pp.529–534: end-to-end test boundaries and vertical/horizontal scenarios. Unit, behavioral and retrieval tests are review.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Build and test Python in GitHub Actions. Consult the Install pinned deps; lint/types if adopted; tests; fixed eval; artifacts sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Fail a PR/commit check on a deliberately broken ownership test and retain the report.

Completion check: A deliberately broken ownership/eval fixture causes a failed CI check.

Alternative · AR video (optional)

3. Version and release metadata

Learn: Code/model/prompt/corpus/index/eval identifiers; config diff.

Required reading:

  • B37 · C10 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B39 · F12 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Documentation tutorial: Managing releases. Read Creating a release, Editing a release and Comparing releases. Attach your own prompt/model/corpus/evaluation manifest; GitHub release documentation does not define AI artifact versioning.

Practice: Attach a version manifest to a demo/evaluation result so it can be reproduced.

Completion check: The release manifest identifies code, model, prompt, corpus/index and eval versions.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

4. Tracing and metrics

Learn: Spans; retrieval latency; provider timing; errors; token use; redaction.

Required reading:

  • B39a · C10 — Read Now. Printed pp.465–471: monitoring/observability. Orchestration pp.472–473 was B37 and is review only.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Python instrumentation. Consult the Spans; retrieval latency; provider timing; errors; token use; redaction sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Trace upload and question flows; create a dashboard or query for errors, latency and usage.

Completion check: A safe trace links stages; latency/error/usage queries work without raw journals.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Deployment and safe operation

Learn: Health/readiness; migration order; worker limits; secret injection.

Required reading:

  • B39 · F12 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Single video (lesson within playlist): mini-RAG · App Deployments 1/2. Only the linked “App Deployments 1/2” video; not the entire mini-RAG playlist. Use the matching branch from the instructor index if comparing code.

Practice: Deploy one small environment and document startup, migration and dependency-outage behavior.

Completion check: Startup, migrations and dependency outages have documented behavior.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Recovery and incident handling

Learn: Restore; release rollback; runbook; retention; injected failure.

Required reading:

  • B39a · C10 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: PostgreSQL backup and restore. Read pg_dump, restoring the dump, and handling large databases only as needed. Perform a database restore drill; application rollback/runbook policies are your own operational exercise.

Practice: Restore a backup into a fresh DB and write a short incident report for an induced failure.

Completion check: A fresh restore succeeds and an injected incident has a written follow-up.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Explain failure boundaries and why a modular monolith is sufficient.
    • Pass: Sensitive content is redacted; representative failures can be located from a trace.
    • Pass: Model or prompt changes trigger an explicit regression review.
    • Pass: Record an actual restore, not just a backup command.
    • Pass: Test outage and rollback; public access requires HTTPS/auth/secrets controls.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S6. Reproduce a release, restore state, diagnose failure, and state operating limits. Docker alone is not production readiness.

Keep: Runbook, CI, release manifest, and traces; Deployment/local operating demo plus restore evidence.

What to defer

  • Kubernetes or multiple observability products by default
  • A GPU cluster before a measured serving requirement

Technical Details · L14

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Application readiness: API, database and worker run locally with known setup commands.
  • Test readiness: Fast deterministic suite and bounded eval cases run independently.
  • Ops reasoning: Distinguish liveness, readiness, migration and dependency failure.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Containers and service topology: API/worker/db; images; networks; volumes; configuration; health.
  • CI and evaluation gates: Install pinned deps; lint/types if adopted; tests; fixed eval; artifacts.
  • Version and release metadata: Code/model/prompt/corpus/index/eval identifiers; config diff.
  • Tracing and metrics: Spans; retrieval latency; provider timing; errors; token use; redaction.
  • Deployment and safe operation: Health/readiness; migration order; worker limits; secret injection.
  • Recovery and incident handling: Restore; release rollback; runbook; retention; injected failure.

Common mistakes and debugging

Container runs therefore production-ready

Test persistence, health, secrets, worker restart and restore.

Only log final model text

Trace parser/retriever/provider stages while redacting private content.

Deploy with no recovery plan

Test restore/rollback and define who can apply a migration.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. What belongs in a trace?
  2. What must be versioned?
  3. Backup equals restore?
Answer guide

What belongs in a trace?

A correlation ID, stage timing, selected model/config and safe error/usage attributes; avoid raw private journals.

What must be versioned?

Code, model, prompt, corpus/index, embedding/chunk policy and evaluation set.

Backup equals restore?

No; a backup is evidence only after a successful restore and consistency check.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N01: Modern Software Engineering & Git Collaboration — official published module title.
  • N05: Software Testing & Full-Stack Debugging — official published module title.
  • N09: Software Architecture & System Design — official published module title.
  • N10: Cloud-Native Deployment & Platform Mapping — official published module title.
  • N15: MLOps Basics & Model Serving — official published module title.
  • N25: LLMOps & Production Readiness for AI Applications — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

The assessed exercise criteria are in the guided page. For evidence use EVIDENCE and EXPERIMENT.

L15 · Model Selection, Cost, Latency, and User Feedback

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 5 · 12–18 focused hours · Difficulty 4/5 · P2

You will learn: Improve a measured bottleneck without sacrificing quality or isolation. Interpret user feedback as a signal rather than unquestioned labels.

Before starting: L05, L14 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B40 · C4 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Model selection for the product

Learn: Language; task quality; license; privacy; context; availability.

Required reading:

  • B40 · C4 — Read Now. Printed pp.179–200: model selection. Cost/latency pp.177–178 are B16 review.

Primary · Book section: B40 · C4. Follow the assigned portion above; use the library references during implementation.

Practice: Compare two feasible models on the same queries and configuration table.

Completion check: Both model candidates use the same corpus, prompts and held-out task definition.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

2. Latency and throughput

Learn: TTFT; end-to-end p50/p95; concurrency; stage budgets.

Required reading:

  • B42 · C9 — Read Now. Printed pp.412–418: performance metrics. Rest of C9 is Optional Advanced in L17.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: Locust quickstart. Consult the TTFT; end-to-end p50/p95; concurrency; stage budgets sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Measure cold/warm runs and concurrency with a fixed workload; show bottleneck spans.

Completion check: Cold/warm p50/p95 and concurrency/sample counts are stated.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

3. Cost accounting

Learn: Input/output tokens; retries; embeddings; reranking; local hardware assumptions.

Required reading:

  • B42 · C9 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Book section: B42 · C9. Follow the assigned portion above; use the library references during implementation.

Practice: Report cost/request using explicit dated assumptions and a hard experiment budget.

Completion check: Per-request costs include explicit dated price or hardware assumptions.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Safe caching

Learn: Cache key; tenant boundary; source/version invalidation; expiry.

Required reading:

  • B41 · F10 — Read Now. PDF pp.429–476: choose caching/performance sections addressing your measured bottleneck; do not implement every technique.

Primary · Book section: B41 · F10. Follow the assigned portion above; use the library references during implementation.

Practice: Test similar questions from two users and deleted sources before enabling a cache.

Completion check: Cache tests separate tenants and invalidate changed/deleted sources.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

5. Measured optimization

Learn: Prompt/context reduction; search tuning; caching; queue/backpressure.

Required reading:

  • B41 · F10 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official repository / implementation guide: pgvector README. Read pgvector’s Querying, Indexing, Filtering and Hybrid Search sections relevant to a measured retrieval bottleneck. Test one change; provider caching/backpressure are separate techniques from F10, not pgvector features.

Practice: Change one bottleneck factor and retain before/after quality, latency and cost results.

Completion check: The one-factor optimization passes existing quality/security gates.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Feedback and monitoring loop

Learn: Consent; ratings; corrections; failure review; feedback bias.

Required reading:

  • B43 · C10 — Read Now. Printed pp.480–491: feedback design and limitations. Do not restart architecture/observability sections.

Primary · Single lecture video + slides: UX for Language User Interfaces. Only the linked “UX for Language User Interfaces” lecture; not the complete LLM Bootcamp. Use the parts relevant to Feedback and monitoring loop. Exact timestamps are not assigned.

Practice: Capture optional feedback, triage three cases and propose a measured follow-up experiment.

Completion check: Three feedback cases have consented signals and a proposed testable follow-up.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Report case count, quality, usage/cost basis, p50/p95, TTFT, date, and environment.
    • Pass: A latency win that breaks citation quality is not presented as an overall improvement.
    • Pass: Test cross-user isolation and deletion before enabling the cache.
    • Pass: Define a review loop; dislike is not automatically a factual-error training label.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S6. Every decision has a baseline, configuration, sample size, and limitations. Similarity is not answer confidence and feedback is not automatically ground truth.

Keep: Model scorecard, controlled optimization report, and feedback taxonomy.

What to defer

  • Semantic caching before invalidation rules
  • Many-model routing or GPU tuning without a bottleneck

python scripts/progress.py set L15 completed --evidence "link-to-commit-and-test-report"

Resource Library · L15 — Model Selection, Cost, Latency, and User Feedback

← Follow the learning path · Technical details · Master book order

This is the complete retained library, not a second to-do list. Follow Primary resources in the README; everything else is an Alternative, Reference or Optional Advanced resource. You do not need to watch every video or finish every course.

Selection key

  • Primary: used by at least one numbered step; follow only its assigned portion.
  • Alternative: optional Arabic/English explanation or practice replacing a primary explanation.
  • Reference: consult a named API/spec section when implementing.
  • Optional Advanced: a later deep dive; does not block this Level.

Resource Type describes the format, independently of its role. A single video containing a playlist parameter remains a single video. Exact titles/numbers are used where verified; unverified identifiers are explicitly marked.

Arabic availability: Arabic lifecycle and deployment material provides context. Dedicated Arabic instruction for controlled model selection, tail latency and cache isolation was not verified; use C4/C9/C10 and the English engineering material.

Book references

Assigned reading is authoritative in the master order for L15. The chapter map preserves full chapter/page locations and split reasons. This library does not assign those chapters again.

Technical Details · L15

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Baseline readiness: Quality, citation, security and latency results have version metadata.
  • Observability readiness: Measure stage timing and usage without private content logging.
  • Selection discipline: Hold corpus/prompt/eval fixed for model comparison.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Model selection for the product: Language; task quality; license; privacy; context; availability.
  • Latency and throughput: TTFT; end-to-end p50/p95; concurrency; stage budgets.
  • Cost accounting: Input/output tokens; retries; embeddings; reranking; local hardware assumptions.
  • Safe caching: Cache key; tenant boundary; source/version invalidation; expiry.
  • Measured optimization: Prompt/context reduction; search tuning; caching; queue/backpressure.
  • Feedback and monitoring loop: Consent; ratings; corrections; failure review; feedback bias.

Common mistakes and debugging

Leaderboard wins equal product wins

Evaluate your bilingual corpus and workload under actual constraints.

Global cache key

Include ownership, model/prompt/corpus versions and deletion invalidation.

Thumbs-up equals ground truth

Feedback is biased and sparse; review failure examples before changing behavior.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. Why p95 as well as p50?
  2. Cost per request?
  3. When optimize?
Answer guide

Why p95 as well as p50?

Median hides slow tail experiences; report concurrency and sample count too.

Cost per request?

Measure input/output tokens and provider prices or local compute assumptions, plus retries/reranking when relevant.

When optimize?

After a reproducible bottleneck is identified and a quality/permission regression gate exists.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N20: AI Evaluation, Safety & Responsible AI — official published module title.
  • N25: LLMOps & Production Readiness for AI Applications — official published module title.

L16 · Capstone Release, Technical Defense, and Career Evidence

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 5 · 12–18 focused hours · Difficulty 4/5 · P2

You will learn: Demonstrate a usable AI application with evaluation and operating evidence. Explain and modify the implementation independently and communicate honest limitations.

Before starting: L02, L03, L08, L09, L10, L11, L12, L13, L14, L15 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

1. Reproducible capstone release

Primary · Documentation article: About repository READMEs. Consult the Setup; seed corpus; secrets template; migrations; version manifest sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: A reviewer runs the release on synthetic data from a clean checkout.

Completion check: A reviewer can run the release with synthetic data from a clean checkout.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

2. Final evaluation and limitations

Learn: Untouched holdout; raw counts; source/citation results; failure taxonomy.

Required reading:

  • B44 · C4 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Documentation: assertions reference: Assertions and metrics. The assertion type matching Final evaluation and limitations; start with deterministic checks, then calibrated model grading if needed. Do not adopt all plugins.

Practice: Freeze the config, run holdout once for release assessment and analyze three failures.

Completion check: The frozen holdout report discloses raw counts and three major failures.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

3. Architecture and technical defense

Primary · Template / reference repository: Architecture decision record templates. Consult the ERD; request trace; ADR; provider boundary; agent/no-agent rationale sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Present a 5-minute demo and a deeper 20-minute walkthrough with one rejected option.

Completion check: The short demo and deeper defense explain one rejected design.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

4. AI fundamentals interview bridge

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Include failure paths and cross-user access tests.
    • Pass: Analyze three major failures and disclose remaining limitations.
    • Pass: A reviewer can reproduce the demo with synthetic data.
    • Pass: Explain framework, model, retrieval, and single-agent decisions.
    • Pass: Only measured figures are used; discuss data ethics, delivery scope, and communication.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S7. Run, explain, modify, and evaluate the system. This is a self-study gate, not an ITI credential or hiring guarantee. L17/L18 remain optional.

Keep: Versioned release, demo, evaluation report/dashboard, and defense notes.

What to defer

  • Waiting for every advanced topic before applying
  • Inflated metrics or unsupported production-ready claims

Resource Library · L16 — Capstone Release, Technical Defense, and Career Evidence

← Follow the learning path · Technical details · Master book order

This is the complete retained library, not a second to-do list. Follow Primary resources in the README; everything else is an Alternative, Reference or Optional Advanced resource. You do not need to watch every video or finish every course.

Selection key

  • Primary: used by at least one numbered step; follow only its assigned portion.
  • Alternative: optional Arabic/English explanation or practice replacing a primary explanation.
  • Reference: consult a named API/spec section when implementing.
  • Optional Advanced: a later deep dive; does not block this Level.

Resource Type describes the format, independently of its role. A single video containing a playlist parameter remains a single video. Exact titles/numbers are used where verified; unverified identifiers are explicitly marked.

Arabic availability: Arabic project orientation and UX/lifecycle lessons support presentation. A verified Arabic AI-engineering interview bank was not found. The Level self-checks and mock defense are recommendations, not official ITI interview questions.

Technical Details · L16

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Core project readiness: Upload, ask, cite, journal connection, history and deletion work end-to-end.
  • Evidence readiness: CI, holdout, security matrix and traces have reproducible artifacts.
  • Independence: Modify and test a small feature without generated code.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Reproducible capstone release: Setup; seed corpus; secrets template; migrations; version manifest.
  • Final evaluation and limitations: Untouched holdout; raw counts; source/citation results; failure taxonomy.
  • Architecture and technical defense: ERD; request trace; ADR; provider boundary; agent/no-agent rationale.
  • AI fundamentals interview bridge: ML leakage; gradients; tokens; embeddings; retrieval; sampling.
  • Backend and operations defense: Auth; SQL; transaction; async; worker; deployment; trace diagnosis.
  • Career evidence and communication: Role-specific CV; scoped proposal; ethical claims; status reporting.

Common mistakes and debugging

Another demo without evidence

Link reproducible tests, datasets, configs and failure analysis.

Production claim exceeds deployment

Describe what was exercised and remaining limits precisely.

Study every optional resource before applying

Use the role checklist and apply after demonstrable core competence.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. How defend a rejected technology?
  2. What makes a CV metric credible?
  3. How present failure?
Answer guide

How defend a rejected technology?

State the requirement, options, evidence, tradeoff and trigger for reconsidering the decision.

What makes a CV metric credible?

A reproducible measurement with workload, sample size, environment and configuration.

How present failure?

Show the case, root cause, mitigation, verification and remaining limitation.

L17 · Fine-Tuning, Dataset Engineering, and Advanced Serving

← Roadmap · Full resource library · Technical details · Book order · Progress

Tier 6 · 18–32 focused hours · Difficulty 4/5 · P3 · Optional Level

You will learn: Choose between prompt changes, RAG, model selection, and fine-tuning using failure evidence. Understand SFT/LoRA, curation, quantization, and serving tradeoffs.

Before starting: L02, L03, L05, L15 Pass the quick diagnostic; review only demonstrated gaps.

Start Here / Recommended Learning Path

Next action: Open B45 · C7 for the assigned portion below, then follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Follow these steps in order. Read each new book assignment once; later mentions explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. Master book order is the authoritative schedule; chapter mapping is the lookup index.

1. Fine-tuning decision

Learn: Prompt versus RAG versus model switch versus adaptation.

Required reading:

  • B45 · C7 — Optional Advanced. Printed pp.311–318: When to Finetune first; select remaining tactics only for a justified adapter experiment.

Primary · Book section: B45 · C7. Follow the assigned portion above; use the library references during implementation.

Practice: Write a go/no-go ADR with the failure cases and cheaper alternatives already tested.

Completion check: The go/no-go decision names actual failures and cheaper alternatives already tried.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

2. Dataset engineering

Learn: Representative tasks; quality; deduplication; consent/license; split boundaries.

Required reading:

  • B46 · C8 — Optional Advanced. Training-data curation sections within printed pp.363–403 as needed; pp.368–371 and 396–402 are B07/B22 review.

Primary · Book section: B46 · C8. Follow the assigned portion above; use the library references during implementation.

Practice: Create a small data card, split-by-source policy and duplicate/leakage checks.

Completion check: Training and holdout data are separated with duplicate/leakage checks.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional)

3. PEFT and SFT

Learn: LoRA/adapters; target modules; data format; training config; checkpoints.

Required reading:

  • B45 · C7 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B46 · C8 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: PEFT quicktour. Consult the LoRA/adapters; target modules; data format; training config; checkpoints sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Run one bounded small-model adapter experiment and save configuration and provenance.

Completion check: A bounded run saves adapter configuration and source provenance.

Alternative · AR machine-translated text (optional) · Implementation reference

4. Holdout and regression evaluation

Learn: Task improvement; general behavior; safety/grounding regressions.

Required reading:

  • B15 · C3 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.
  • B16 · C4 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Documentation: assertions reference: Assertions and metrics. The assertion type matching Holdout and regression evaluation; start with deterministic checks, then calibrated model grading if needed. Do not adopt all plugins.

Practice: Compare tuned/untuned models on an independent set and report negative outcomes too.

Completion check: The independent comparison reports regressions as well as gains.

5. Quantization and inference

Learn: Precision; model memory; hardware; batching; throughput versus latency.

Required reading:

  • B47 · C9 — Optional Advanced. Selected serving/optimization sections within printed pp.405–447 beyond the B42 metrics slice. F5 PDF240–252 is an optional companion for batching/KV/paged attention.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: bitsandbytes quantization. Read bitsandbytes prerequisites and the 8-bit/4-bit loading examples. Record hardware/model constraints and compare measured memory/quality; batching is covered separately in the optional serving reading.

Practice: Benchmark memory/latency/quality before and after one compatible optimization.

Completion check: Memory, speed and task quality are measured on compatible hardware.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

6. Model-serving boundary

Learn: Separate runtime; adapter deployment; version rollback; lifecycle cost.

Required reading:

  • B47 · C9 — Previously Covered / Review Only. Reuse only the part needed for this step; do not restart the chapter.

Primary · Official documentation / tutorial: vLLM quickstart. Consult the Separate runtime; adapter deployment; version rollback; lifecycle cost sections/examples only. Do not read the entire documentation site.

Practice: Serve the chosen artifact separately and prove a rollback to the baseline model.

Completion check: The serving boundary can roll back to the untuned baseline.

Alternative · AR video (optional) · Implementation reference

Practical exercises

The step tasks feed the assessed exercises below; combine them in the same application rather than creating extra projects.

    • Pass: Choose not to fine-tune when the failure evidence does not justify it.
    • Pass: Private journals are not implicitly repurposed as training data.
    • Pass: Compare a prompt baseline on independent holdout; otherwise deliver a resource-aware experiment plan.
    • Pass: Report memory/latency/quality on the actual hardware; no invented runs.

Ready to move on?

Capstone checkpoint: S7. A conceptual decision can be completed without a GPU. Practical training is not marked done without run evidence. This module is optional for Core completion.

Keep: Customization decision, dataset card, and experiment plan; Run report only if a training/serving experiment was actually executed.

What to defer

  • Training a foundation model
  • Paying for compute without a hypothesis and evaluation

Unlocks: No further required Level; return to the capstone or role checklist.

Technical Details · L17

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Use this page when checking readiness, diagnosing a mistake or reviewing curriculum provenance. The ordered learning instructions and book assignments live in the Level README and master book order.

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • Failure evidence: Show a task failure that prompting, retrieval or model choice did not solve.
  • Training readiness: Explain loss, optimizer, leakage and held-out evaluation.
  • Budget readiness: Choose hardware/runtime and stop conditions before any training run.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • Fine-tuning decision: Prompt versus RAG versus model switch versus adaptation.
  • Dataset engineering: Representative tasks; quality; deduplication; consent/license; split boundaries.
  • PEFT and SFT: LoRA/adapters; target modules; data format; training config; checkpoints.
  • Holdout and regression evaluation: Task improvement; general behavior; safety/grounding regressions.
  • Quantization and inference: Precision; model memory; hardware; batching; throughput versus latency.
  • Model-serving boundary: Separate runtime; adapter deployment; version rollback; lifecycle cost.

Common mistakes and debugging

Fine-tuning for changing factual documents

RAG may be a better mechanism; demonstrate the actual adaptation need.

Train/eval near-duplicates

Split by source or task group and audit leakage.

Quantization improves everything

Measure memory, speed and quality; hardware compatibility varies.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. When fine-tune?
  2. What is LoRA?
  3. What must compare?
Answer guide

When fine-tune?

When a stable task/behavior gap and sufficient representative data justify training and maintenance costs.

What is LoRA?

A parameter-efficient adaptation method using trainable low-rank updates instead of full-weight training.

What must compare?

The same independent task cases before/after tuning, including regressions, memory, latency and cost.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N12: Data Preparation & Feature Engineering — official published module title.
  • N15: MLOps Basics & Model Serving — official published module title.
  • N21: Fine-Tuning & Model Customization — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Learning objectives and evidence

The assessed exercise criteria are in the guided page. For evidence use EVIDENCE and EXPERIMENT.

Tier 6 · 12–20 focused hours · Difficulty 2/5 · P3 · Optional Level

Next action: follow Step 1’s Primary resource and do its task. Other resources are choices, not a completion list.

Technical Details · L18

← Guided learning path · Resource library · ITI coverage

Prerequisites and quick diagnostic

  • JavaScript transfer: Use promises, modules and HTTP requests without a full JS course.
  • Backend readiness: Stable auth, upload, question and source endpoints exist.
  • Product contract: Define streaming events and the citation response schema.

Use existing CRM evidence where applicable. A gap triggers targeted review, not a beginner-course restart.

Complete technical scope

  • TypeScript bridge: Interfaces; unions; narrowing; request/response types.
  • React state and components: Upload state; chat turns; journal form; component boundaries.
  • Authenticated API client: Fetch; cookies/headers; expired sessions; server errors.
  • Streaming and cancellation: ReadableStream; event framing; abort; partial completion.
  • Evidence and reflection UX: Source/page view; book claim versus suggestion; uncertainty; memory controls.
  • Accessibility and end-to-end tests: Keyboard; focus; labels; errors; upload→ask→source→delete.

Common mistakes and debugging

Full frontend specialization

Implement only the capstone user flows.

Client guards replace backend auth

Server ownership tests remain mandatory.

Render model output as arbitrary HTML

Use safe rendering and explicit source links; avoid unsanitized HTML.

Self-check

These are original reasoning prompts, not confirmed ITI questions.

  1. Why a discriminated union for API results?
  2. Why AbortController?
  3. What proves usable citations?
Answer guide

Why a discriminated union for API results?

It forces success/error/stream states to be handled explicitly.

Why AbortController?

It cancels a fetch/stream and lets the UI stop unnecessary work.

What proves usable citations?

Keyboard-accessible source links resolve to authorized passages with page/context and clear loading/error states.

ITI provenance and industry extensions

  • N05: Software Testing & Full-Stack Debugging — official published module title.
  • N08: Frontend TypeScript & React — official published module title.

Detailed subtopics, specific tools, labs, prerequisites, learning hours and selected resources are roadmap recommendations, not an official ITI reading list. The coverage matrix remains the syllabus record.

Evaluation and Evidence Standards

Home · L05 · Experiment template

Primary references: C3–4, C6, and F11. Evaluate components separately; HTTP200 does not establish answer quality. The following are project recommendations, not official ITI thresholds.

Dataset rule Application
Public seed Twelve supplied synthetic cases are practice data, never unseen holdout
Development Author approximately 30 reviewed cases covering normal and failure behavior
Final holdout Keep approximately 20 additional cases independent of tuning
Labels Query, answerability, expected facts, document/page/span evidence, category, and language
Human review Do not accept model-generated labels or answers as gold without review
Changes Preserve source-level labels when chunk IDs change
Component Measure Important distinction
Parsing Text/page correctness and explicit failure reasons Empty or scanned PDF is not a successful empty index
Retrieval Hit@k and recall@k Any required evidence versus the fraction of all required evidence retrieved
Generation Correctness, groundedness, and relevance on 0–2 rubrics Use oracle context to isolate generation failure
Citations Valid IDs and actual claim support An existing ID may still support nothing in the answer
Abstention Appropriate no-answer and false-refusal rates Refusing every query is not success
Agents Task success, tool/argument errors, limit violations, and unauthorized attempts Evaluate actions and outcomes, not just final prose
Context Required facts retained, stale memory, and distractor effects Longer context is not automatically better
Serving p50/p95, TTFT, error rate, usage, and cost basis Report workload, environment, sample count, and date
Check type Dependencies What it establishes
Unit/API Fake providers Contracts, branches, failures, and validation
Integration Real test database/retriever Ownership, persistence, metadata, and component boundaries
Live evaluation Actual model with a separate budget Model/application behavior under versioned inputs
Model judge Calibrated against human review Helpful imperfect scoring, not sole ground truth

Keep a baseline and change one variable per experiment. Negative findings are valid evidence. Small latency samples are descriptive, not a general reliability benchmark. Record the source/date of prices rather than inventing current costs.

Context Engineering

Home · L09 · L12

Anthropic’s article motivates deliberate context selection and maintenance, retrieval when needed, and strategies for long-running work. The applications below are proposed project exercises, supported by C5 context efficiency and C6 memory; they are not mandatory steps from the article.

Concept Module Project exercise
Prompt versus context L04/L09 Separate instructions from evidence, history, journals, and memory
Context rot L09 Compare focused context against distracting material
Selection L08/L09 Budget and prioritize retrieved evidence and recent turns
Just-in-time retrieval L12 Load a permitted passage through a bounded tool
Tool design L12 Clear schemas, concise results, and useful errors
Compaction L09 Test retention of required facts before/after summarization
Structured memory L09 Owner/source/date with correction and deletion
Sub-agents L12 Optional comparison using isolated context and a summary contract
Product boundary Requirement
Journals The user chooses what enters context
Memory Editable, sourced, dated, and scoped; avoid automatic permanent personality conclusions
Summaries Preserve covered message ranges and evidence provenance; stale summaries may be wrong
Authorization The server authorizes access before loading content into context
Testing Measure lost facts, stale values, contradictory entries, and deletion behavior

Security and Responsible AI

Home · L11 · Threat model

References: F8–9, C5, and OWASP GenAI. Documents, journals, and tool outputs are untrusted inputs. RAG or fine-tuning does not automatically eliminate prompt injection.

Boundary Required behavior Evidence
Identity Derive the user on the server, never from model-chosen authority A/B access tests
Retrieval Filter ownership before context construction Cross-user search and source-viewer tests
Tools Least privilege and server-side argument/permission checks Unknown/unauthorized tool tests
Writes Appropriate approval and idempotency Simulated repeated-write/failure tests
Consumption Bound upload/pages/tokens/requests/steps/retries Oversized and abuse cases
Secrets Keep keys out of repository, browser, prompts, and logs Configuration/log review
Journals Do not log sensitive contents by default Redaction and retention review
Deletion Remove active derived content and document backup retention Delete/re-query/context/cache tests
Deployment HTTPS, appropriate CORS/cookie/bearer/CSRF choices Public-demo gate
Before public access Status to record
API/source/stream/job authorization Tested with multiple users
Provider outage and partial stream failure Tested
Persistence and restore Demonstrated
Demo inputs and budgets Synthetic and bounded
Residual risk Documented; passing designed tests is not universal proof of security

Effort and Pacing

Home · Progress

The comprehensive Core is approximately 233–355 focused hours before diagnostic waivers. These are personal learning estimates, not ITI hours or an obligation to finish everything within twelve weeks.

Availability Adaptation
15–20 hours/week Approximately12–24 weeks for Core; S3 produces a demonstrable RAG application earlier
8–10 hours/week Extend the calendar while retaining prerequisites and security/evaluation gates
25+ hours/week Spend extra time on failure analysis and review rather than adding frameworks
Example18-hour allocation 4 reading,9 building,3 tests/evaluation/docs,2 foundations or review; adjust as needed
Situation Action
Familiar backend topic Pass a diagnostic and a small Python transfer exercise; skim repeated theory
Whole-module waiver Demonstrate every gate, record evidence and reason, and keep it visible as waived
Stuck implementation Record a blocker, reduce to one behavior, and read to answer a specific question
Independent foundations Progress L02 alongside implementation; do not silently remove it from Core
Early job application Use actual milestones and existing CRM evidence; do not wait for every optional topic

Completing one familiar subtopic does not waive an entire module. ORM lifecycle, model evaluation, and LLM security may remain new even with substantial backend experience.

Progress Tracker

Action Command

Find eligible work python scripts/progress.py next
Start a module python scripts/progress.py set L00 in_progress
Record completion python scripts/progress.py set L00 completed --evidence "progress/evidence/m00.md"

Reopen work python scripts/progress.py set L00 not_started
Status Rule
not_started No completion claimed
in_progress Hard prerequisites must be accepted
completed Prerequisites and an evidence field are required
waived Prerequisites, evidence, and a diagnostic reason are required
blocked A reason is required; use it to document a real blocker

Run commands from the repository root. Reopening a prerequisite is rejected while an active descendant depends on it; reopen descendants first. Manual CSV edits can be checked with validate.py. Tool-generated timestamps use UTC.

The set command refreshes PROGRESS.md automatically for the default tracker. After manual CSV edits, run export. The HTML overview is a build-time snapshot; rebuild it if you want its progress tables refreshed.

The CLI checks sequence and fields, not skill quality or whether a report proves its claims. Review evidence yourself. L17/L18 do not block Core completion. No learning outcomes have been completed on your behalf.

Maintaining the roadmap

Edit the JSON sources, then run:

python scripts/render_learning.py
python scripts/audit_level_content.py
python scripts/build_overview.py
python scripts/validate.py
python -m unittest discover -s tests -v

Only the optional HTML rebuild requires Pandoc. The Level audit checks generated pages against a fresh isolated render. It also detects removed research, changed gates/prerequisites, broken Markdown fragments and inconsistent reading IDs. Update the preservation ledger only for an intentional, reviewed curriculum change; do not regenerate it just to silence a failure.

For live external URL rechecking run python scripts/audit_resources.py (network required). For a portable copy run python scripts/package_repo.py output/roadmap.zip; Git metadata, caches, secrets and book PDFs are excluded. OVERVIEW.html is a standalone optional reader; GitHub Markdown is the primary experience.

Use python scripts/progress.py next to choose unlocked work. Learner progress is not altered by rendering. Keep personal journals and copyrighted book text out of this repository.

Learning with AI Assistance

Home

Stage Your responsibility
Attempt Work for25–40 minutes and describe expected behavior, observations, and errors
Ask Request a hint, explanation, or focused review rather than an entire implementation
Implement Write, run, and test the solution yourself
Verify understanding Explain the code and make a small requirement change without copying
Review labels Human-review evaluation labels and important decisions
Protect data Understand what is sent to a service; never include secrets or private journals casually
Record evidence Identify what you built, what failed, why you chose an approach, and how to reproduce it

A working solution you cannot explain is still a learning gap. AI may review a test; it does not turn unreviewed model output into ground truth.

Resource selection and verification policy

Roadmap · Level audit · Book order · Chapter locator

How the library is curated

Decision Rule
Topic first A resource must teach a named topic, supply a relevant implementation reference, or support a specific exercise. A channel homepage is not used as a substitute for a lesson.
One practical route The owned book/article, one explanation and the actual project exercise form the path. Extra courses are alternatives, not cumulative prerequisites.
Arabic teaching Identify the instructor and specific lesson where available. Egyptian mini-RAG, Python, ML and TypeScript teaching is distinguished from translated text.
Arabic translations Microsoft translations identify Co-op Translator. They are labelled machine-translated text; the linked English video is not called Arabic instruction.
Partial coverage When an Arabic lesson supplies only prerequisites or orientation, the topic row states that boundary. A missing exact resource is recorded as a gap.
English teaching Prefer course/lecture pages containing real videos, notebooks or exercises. Current official docs resolve API/version differences.
Paid alternatives Recommend only a clearly relevant alternative with an explicit reason to buy. No new paid course is necessary for completion.
Practice Distinguish self-assessed local tasks from platform quizzes, automated checks and hosted labs. A concept quiz does not assess a working system.
Advanced material Papers and deeper serving/agent patterns follow demonstrated need. They do not block the first working application.

What was verified

The discovery process inspected author/publisher course outlines, instructor lesson indexes, official documentation pages and relevant content excerpts. For mini-RAG, the instructor’s tut-017 README lists direct video titles and matching code branches; the older main README stops earlier. Hesham Asem’s own course index identifies the ML playlists. Elzero’s study plans identify lesson subjects and assignments. DeepLearning.AI pages show lesson outlines and access terms. Microsoft Arabic lesson pages were opened after correcting their translation paths.

The final HTTP audit issues GET requests, follows redirects and records status, final URL, page title and check date. The machine-readable evidence is in RESOURCE_VERIFICATION.json. This verifies reachability at a point in time; a 200 response alone does not prove instructional quality. Topic assignment was separately reviewed against the source’s title, outline or documentation scope.

Verification limits

  • Not every video was watched end-to-end. Arabic video topic identity is supported by instructor-authored lesson/course indexes and reachable lesson URLs; playable media can still vary by account, region or later removal.
  • Paid enrollments and account-only assessments were not purchased or completed. Regional prices are not invented. Auto-generated Arabic subtitles do not make an English course an Arabic course.
  • DeepLearning.AI advertises limited-time free course access and separately promotes Pro accomplishments. Access and assessment terms can change; the official/local alternative remains available.
  • Historical courses can teach valid architecture while showing obsolete dependencies. Specific cautions are retained in the resource cards.
  • Book assignments use the three supplied PDFs’ verified contents and page conventions. Books are referenced, not redistributed. No additional chapter numbers were guessed.
  • These resources and practice questions are recommendations. They are not an official ITI reading list or confirmed interview questions.

Recheck after editing

python scripts/audit_level_content.py
python scripts/audit_resources.py
python scripts/validate.py

The content audit runs offline. The resource audit needs network access and reports failures rather than silently replacing a URL or treating a login wall as proof of full access. Review failures before changing a curated assignment.

Learning selection in this edition

Resource Type and Role answer different questions. Type says what the link is (one video, playlist, full/short course, documentation, article or lab); Role says how to use it in this Level (Primary, Alternative, Reference or Optional Advanced). The README chooses one Primary per step. The full library is preserved without requiring all alternatives.

Assigned portions name verified lessons or sections where available. For Elzero and the mini-RAG instructor index, identified lesson numbers/titles are retained. DeepLearning.AI uses named lessons, not invented timestamps. Where individual playlist identifiers could not be confirmed, the card says so and gives a subject boundary; those playlists are alternatives. Documentation assignments distinguish actual source APIs from engineering decisions you must design yourself.

Only reading_plan.json assigns new book reading. The master order and Level pages are rendered from it; the chapter map is a locator. Original topic-to-book associations remain research metadata, not additional homework. Later appearances explicitly say Previously Covered / Review Only. A chapter split identifies bounded sections and one first-use owner for each.

For the new information architecture and its all-Level checks see Learning Experience Audit. The dated HTTP catalog report is preserved as historical verification; this refactor does not claim every course or video was replayed.

Reference-Level Comparison and Revision

Roadmap · Audit

Structural reference: Yahia Sherif’s Data Engineering Roadmap 2026, inspected at commit 4ba9d36fbfc809e7ebb087e651e1df86aea6f2bc. The following are comparable engineering roles in the curriculum, not claims that AI and Data Engineering subjects are interchangeable. Representative purpose Reference README + resources AI README + resources Finding and implemented change
Foundation/API transfer 04-Python 01-Python-FastAPI Reference connects topics to Arabic and English teaching and gives a categorized resource library. AI L01 previously had four documentation links. It now distinguishes Python remediation, API validation, lifecycle, testing and their matching lessons/references.
Production quality/operations 23-Governance-Quality-Observability 14-LLMOps-Deployment Reference separates teaching, practice, documentation and optional depth and admits Arabic scarcity. AI L14 previously reused Docker links for recovery. It now maps deployment, CI, versions, telemetry and recovery to distinct resources and an actual restore drill.
Capstone/evidence 26-Capstone 16-Capstone-Career Reference treats capstone as an evidence-producing system. AI L16 previously had four broad references. It now supplies release, evaluation, defense, role-specific communication and independent implementation guidance with matched learning resources.

Consistent internal contract

README resources.md
Prerequisites + diagnostic, objectives and selected path Arabic/Egyptian materials; English videos and teaching
Topic/subtopic → Arabic/English → practical output Slides/notebooks/code; paid alternatives
Topic → verified book/article focus + implementation reference Your three books/article; external practice/labs/quizzes
Official ITI titles separated from recommended detail Official docs/engineering; optional advanced material
Assessed exercises, pitfalls and original self-check with answer guide Per-resource provider, language, access, topic assignment and limits
Capstone acceptance, checklist, postpone and tracker navigation Stop rules, duplicate-reading guidance and verification date

The authoring contracts are Level template and resource-library template. All 19 pairs are audited, including later Levels. The reference’s visual grouping and category approach inform the organization; its wording, broad channel links and subject-specific recommendations are not copied.

Learning Experience Refactor Audit

Home · Resource preservation · Reading order

Date: 16 October 2026. Baseline: b788161947af8381f2a385a258d80197bddc7b4a. Scope: information architecture and learning guidance in the existing repository. Six tiers, 19 Levels, syllabus coverage, prerequisites and practical contracts are preserved.

Acceptance criteria

  • Every README exposes its next action before the numbered steps and uses zero resource/reference tables.
  • Every topic connects concepts, bounded reading, one Primary resource, practice and an observable completion check.
  • Every library retains its assigned resource IDs and now distinguishes format, selection role, assigned portion, scope and limits.
  • Diagnostics, full scope, mistakes and self-check answers remain accessible in details.md.
  • The book ledger owns new reading; all step IDs and generated guides agree. Review references introduce no new mandatory chapter restart.
  • Preserved catalog records and topic research, 84 assessed exercises, prerequisites, gates, ITI mappings, eight milestone files and learner progress are mechanically compared with the baseline.

The “next action within 30 seconds” goal is addressed through position and explicit wording; no timed usability study is claimed. A learner still decides how much background review is needed.

All Levels preserve Arabic/Egyptian and English choices. Arabic translated text and prerequisite-only coverage remain explicitly labelled; advanced gaps are not disguised as verified Arabic video courses. Unverified playlist lesson identifiers are disclosed. No full 10–20-hour course is required merely because its URL is listed.

Representative before/after comparison

The comparison uses the actual baseline files, not a reconstructed example. README table counts below measure Markdown table blocks; the complete technical scope was moved into support pages rather than discarded.

Level Before After Effect
L01 10 README table blocks; parallel topic/resource/book instructions 0 README tables; 6 ordered steps plus library/details links Routes → dependencies → Python typing/contracts → cleanup → tests. A learner can transfer Express experience without a full beginner course.
L09 10 README table blocks; parallel topic/resource/book instructions 0 README tables; 6 ordered steps plus library/details links Context rot → selection → JIT retrieval → memory → compaction → sub-agent decision. Anthropic sections are selected step by step; prior C5 is review.
L14 10 README table blocks; parallel topic/resource/book instructions 0 README tables; 6 ordered steps plus library/details links Containers → CI → release manifest → tracing → deployment → recovery. F12 is assigned once; later steps apply it instead of restarting the chapter.

The resource libraries deliberately remain comprehensive. Their repeated resource cards are reference entries, not extra required learning steps. Book sections link one master ledger instead of carrying independent reading assignments in three places. Repeated use of an article or documentation page is limited to the named section or API needed by the next task.

Verification and limits

python scripts/audit_level_content.py performs the all-Level consistency, preservation and Markdown-fragment audit, including isolated re-render comparison. python scripts/validate.py and the existing tooling tests check the prerequisite graph, tracker, source coverage, synthetic data and reader links.

Source outlines and supplied book text were consulted for section boundaries. In particular F5 provider/RAG/concurrency portions and F11 unit/integration/behavioral/E2E portions were separated using the actual text. Full source chapters and research associations remain in the chapter map/catalog. No book PDFs are distributed. The existing dated external catalog check is retained; HTTP reachability and course outlines do not amount to complete video playback or paid assessment verification.

Changelog

Guided learning experience — 16 October 2026

  • Refactored all 19 Level READMEs into ordered topic → reading → Primary resource → task → completion-check journeys.
  • Kept comprehensive Arabic/English libraries, adding explicit resource formats, roles and bounded selections. Alternatives remain optional.
  • Moved diagnostics, debugging, full technical scope and self-check answers into per-Level details.md pages.
  • Added one chronological master book queue and a separate chapter index, with section ownership and explicit review statuses.
  • Preserved the six tiers, prerequisites, ITI coverage, all 153 catalog sources, topic research, 84 assessed exercises, eight capstone milestones and learner progress.
  • Updated entry points, templates, HTML reader and audits to detect lost research, stale generated pages, inconsistent reading and broken internal navigation.

See the all-Level audit for representative before/after comparisons and verification limits.
